A one-row summary of every ADR in this folder. See each record for full context.

| [0012](0012-nine-slice-frames-are-atom-assembled.md) | Scalable 9-slice chrome frames are atom-assembled (corner mirrored → symmetric); whole-frame generation retired for chrome | accepted | 2026-06-26 |
| [0013](0013-transparency-chroma-key-via-subscription.md) | Generated-chrome transparency = chroma-key + despill via the subscription codex; native paid-API path rejected on cost | accepted | 2026-06-26 |
| [0014](0014-ui-chrome-low-fidelity-aesthetic.md) | UI chrome targets the concept's low-fidelity element aesthetic (~few-hundred colors, chunky); forges specify shape + fidelity per ui-chrome-vocabulary.md; never "soft anti-aliased" | accepted | 2026-06-26 |
| [0015](0015-doodads-frame-units-not-bury-them.md) | Doodads frame the unit, not bury it: shin-height foliage that covers feet only; solids aren't stand-inside props; terrain-gated | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0016](0016-single-source-nine-slice-registry.md) | Single-source nine-slice registry: one declaration drives bake, editor, and catalog | accepted; Git-owned installed-registry clauses superseded by ADR-0106 | 2026-06-27 |
| [0017](0017-per-asset-flipsides-handedness.md) | Per-asset `flipSides` handedness so one assembler serves both flat keylines and beveled rails | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0018](0018-variant-states-are-whole-frame-palette-swaps.md) | Active/selected frame states are whole-frame palette swaps (body + border, not just the accent) | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0019](0019-dev-only-nine-slice-editor-save.md) | Dev-only in-app 9-slice editor persistence | accepted; repo-write clauses superseded by ADR-0085 and ADR-0106 | 2026-06-27 |
| [0020](0020-settings-body-typography-role-scale.md) | Settings body text maps to the role→token type scale (eyebrow 2xs · description xs · value/label sm · row title md); the off-scale stepper readout fixed to sm | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0021](0021-settings-button-label-sizing.md) | Settings ACTION buttons (header + panel pills) share ONE body-tier (sm) label, grouped by role not frame; kept below titles; box hugs the label at a 40px desktop height (not the 44-48px touch floor — mouse game); emphasis via color/fill | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0022](0022-settings-nav-tabs-typography.md) | Settings rail tabs are a NAVIGATION component (governed apart from action buttons); sized at the content row-title tier (md), never above the rows they lead to; SETTINGS stays lg as the hierarchy top; supersedes the undocumented 1.25rem tab size from #148 | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0023](0023-app-title-bar-layout-and-controls.md) | App title bars: centered three-section layout + labeled controls on the kit frame (never bare icons; icon+label in-game, text in menus); generalizes ADR-0010 | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0024](0024-ui-typography-system.md) | UI typography is one tokenized system: --ds-font-* families (Jersey 10 dropped) + the --ds-text-* size scale + weight/tracking tokens, mandatory; raw font literals disallowed; migration is staged screen-by-screen | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0025](0025-world-scene-art-anti-story-lore.md) | World/scene/portrait art is governed by the anti-story lore (docs/lore-anti-story.md + lore/historical-anchors + background contracts): residue-not-spectacle, faceless pieces, ordinary life beside an unseen real-historical catastrophe; palette per-image (no forced grade) | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0026](0026-ui-kit-icon-canvas.md) | UI-kit content icons live on one canonical 64×64 centered canvas with a normalized ~40×40 safe area; the forge pads-to-canvas + asserts dimensions (never ships a bare bbox); ratifies the de-facto box 15 icons honor, supersedes the icons.md canvas geometry | accepted; display-scaling clause superseded by [0076](0076-scaling-is-calibration-production-art-is-native-1x.md) | 2026-06-27 |
| [0027](0027-icon-optical-keylines.md) | Shape-varied icon sets (the carved main-menu icons) place each glyph by per-shape OPTICAL keyline within ADR-0026's canonical 64×64 canvas — equal optical mass, not equal margins; optical-centering baked into each PNG's padding; one `--menu-icon-size` token on consumption; re-pack not re-forge | accepted; downscale-without-reforge clauses superseded by [0076](0076-scaling-is-calibration-production-art-is-native-1x.md) | 2026-06-27 |
| [0028](0028-settings-row-symmetric-side-padding.md) | Settings/list row content is inset symmetrically (equal left/right side padding on the `--ds-space` scale); the optional value/control are a right-aligned flex group so the right inset matches the left for every row variant — no fixed grid column that strands a gap (the retired icon's stale 4th column was the lopsiding) | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0029](0029-catalog-category-requirements.md) | Every Studio catalog category meets one requirements contract (grid + Search/Zoom/filters/View-Selected + a destination; read-only ⇒ its own viewerKind + Details readout); closes the hand-rolled-controls hole that let Scrollbars ship with Search only | accepted; visible Viewer-kind selector clause partially superseded by [0342](0342-studio-viewers-are-entered-through-catalog-and-own-only-local-controls.md) | 2026-06-27 |
| [0030](0030-scrollbars-never-vanish.md) | Scrollbars never vanish: an always-present **drawn** rail (a real DOM element — recessed groove even when empty), grip thumb only when scrollable (no false affordance); the native bar is hidden and a shared `<KitScroll>` paints + drives the rail/thumb (cross-browser, screenshot-verifiable, can't be dropped by the engine). Replaces the first native-`::-webkit` skin that vanished on empty panes | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0031](0031-ui-spacing-system.md) | UI spacing is one tokenized system: ratify `--ds-space-*` (extend 48/64), spatial roles (inset/stack/inline/gutter), compact/comfortable density per ADR-0006, raw px on spacing banned; spacing sibling of ADR-0024 | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0032](0032-no-bland-html-css-every-surface-is-kit.md) | No bland HTML/CSS: every VISIBLE surface (panel/box/button/chip/frame) is a kit 9-slice or generated sprite; raw-CSS surfaces banned app-wide; layout containers + text exempt; generalizes ADR-0002/0011/0012/0014 | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0033](0033-board-plus-control-panel-layout.md) | Board/canvas + controls views (skirmish, level editor): clean board floating on the scene (no CSS box), one right-side kit control panel (tabs + content + actions, panel.png matching the title bar); shared precedent | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0034](0034-nine-slice-line-frames-for-surface-fill.md) | A surface behind kit chrome uses a transparent-interior "line" frame (full ornament over a see-through center — assembled from the same atoms with a transparent fill; no masking, no carve) + the surface as a separate border-box background; a surfaced element keeps a frame, but the ornament must reach the element edge (corner brackets do; the inset steel row rail does not, so surfaced rows borrow the bracket frame); never a per-material baked frame; opt-in via a registry `line` field, parity-tested | accepted | 2026-06-27 |
| [0035](0035-semantic-ui-icons-carry-a-private-anti-story-anchor.md) | A semantic UI icon that depicts a world object draws its subject from the ADR-0025 anti-story material vocabulary (not the stock symbol library) and carries a one-line PRIVATE anchor documented in the ADR — for us, never shown to the player; function still reads at a glance. Founding instance: the `sign-out` door = a stripped cloister door anchored to the Dissolution of the Monasteries | accepted | 2026-06-28 |
| [0036](0036-trailing-account-settings-cluster-is-icon-only.md) | The trailing "settings + user" cluster (avatar account-menu trigger + Settings gear) is icon-only — the single bounded exception to ADR-0023's labeled-controls rule; justified by the avatar/gear corner convention and gated on aria-label + hover tooltip + kit frame. Every other header control stays labeled | accepted | 2026-06-28 |
| [0037](0037-title-bar-full-bleed-bottom-rule.md) | The shared app title bar drops the 4-sided `panel-line` frame for a full-bleed bottom RULE — a forged "nailhead" iron band + centred diamond stud (generated low-fi atoms) over edge-to-edge oak; kills the 1px keyline edge gap and matches the top-bar idiom; scopes an exception to ADR-0034 (panels/rails keep their line frames) | accepted | 2026-06-28 |
| [0038](0038-campaigns-are-tiered-game-content.md) | Campaigns are tiered game content: a GLOBAL official tier (committed `official.json` fallback + a live admin-authored `official_campaigns` DB row, public GET / `requireAdmin` PUT via `ADMIN_EMAILS`) always visible to everyone, PLUS the unchanged per-user `campaign_workspaces` tier; hydrate always-loads-then-merges (fixes the signed-in-empty blank screen); DB is authoritative, the file is a weekly git-baked recoverable mirror (model A, nothing to retire); honors no-DB-dependency + content-is-code-owned; the `official.json` fixture + weekly bake were later removed (2026-07-03) — the DB row is the sole source | accepted | 2026-06-28 |
| [0039](0039-tile-top-and-side-are-composable-layers.md) | A board tile keeps TOP and SIDE material as independently selectable layers so side treatments vary without re-baking N×M combinations; disjoint pixels + hard alpha avoid double-darkening. ADR-0087 later replaces its whole-cell/two-image side compositor with independent south/east face composition | accepted; whole-cell side composition superseded by ADR-0087 | 2026-06-28 |
| [0040](0040-feature-tiles-own-geometry-generate-material.md) | Board/feature tile art: own the GEOMETRY (footprint/mask/placement may be computed — like the canonical template + Blender edge), but the MATERIAL/painted look must be properly GENERATED, never code-drawn (flat RGB/gradients); connection tiles (roads→rivers/walls) follow the surface-swap shape (computed masks + generated material stamped in); extends ADR-0011 chrome→board; the currently-shipped code-drawn road is interim/non-production until its material is regenerated | accepted | 2026-06-28 |
| [0041](0041-continuous-board-edges-mural-windows-and-story-features.md) | Continuous board edges: one wide cliff mural per family is sliced into ordered windows so geology flows instead of restarting per tile; story features reuse the side pipeline with a clean cap when clipped. ADR-0087 later makes south/east straight runs and overrides independent rather than wrapping one whole-cell side around the corner | accepted; whole-cell assignment/corner wrap superseded by ADR-0087 | 2026-06-29 |
| [0042](0042-title-bar-is-an-invariant-screens-add-slots.md) | The title bar is an INVARIANT: `AppTitleBar` always renders the brand (leading) + account/settings cluster (trailing); no config can suppress either. Screens only ADD optional regions between them — `centerSlot` (status) + `actionsSlot` (labeled controls, renames `rightSlot`), inserted BEFORE the cluster, never replacing it. Deletes the `showAccountCluster` opt-in (its only honest value was true); `showSettingsGear:false`/`signInReturnTo` are the only cluster modulations (per ADR-0036). Fixes the Level/Campaign editors that had dropped the gear+avatar; enforces ADR-0023's anti-drift intent in code; refines ADR-0023/0036 | accepted; actionsSlot mechanism superseded by [0104](0104-title-bar-controls-are-typed-contributions-to-one-lane.md) | 2026-06-28 |
| [0043](0043-ui-motion-system.md) | UI motion is one tokenized system: TWO durations — `--ds-duration-fast` (100ms, instant hover/toggle feedback) + `--ds-duration-fade` (350ms, EVERY fade/crossfade — one fade speed, not surface-tiered; overruled an earlier base/slow split) — plus easing tokens (`--ds-ease-standard`/`out`/`linear`), mandatory for interactive transitions; raw ms/bezier disallowed. Reduced-motion gates MOVEMENT not opacity (pure fades aren't gated — many players run Windows animations off = Chrome `reduce`). First consumers: settings menu crossfade + screen-entrance fade + bgm-control, all on `--ds-duration-fade`. Motion sibling of ADR-0024/0031; staged migration | accepted | 2026-06-29 |
| [0044](0044-persistent-mute-control-in-the-trailing-cluster.md) | The BGM mute toggle becomes a persistent, icon-only member of the trailing cluster (`[mute] [gear] [avatar]`), reachable top-right on every route — retiring the floating bottom-right `.bgm-control` that was `display:none`'d on the menu/Skirmish/Studio. Same `bgm.js` button re-homed into a boxless `.cluster-bgm-slot` (created detached, placed via MutationObserver since `initBgm` runs before the bar mounts) — one control, one mute state, all cross-tab/retry/now-playing logic untouched. Sanctioned under ADR-0036's three mitigations (aria-label+title, kit `mode-button` frame, unambiguous toggle); refines ADR-0036/0042 | accepted | 2026-06-28 |
| [0045](0045-units-deploy-with-a-staggered-drop-in.md) | Units DEPLOY at board start with a staggered drop-in instead of a flat appear: each spawns hovering above its square, hangs motionless while fading in, then drops under gravity into a thud (impact squash, no bounce). Wave order is communication — player force first (home row→forward off `startY`), enemy answers, each wave ending on its royal; rocks don't drop. Presentation-only (never gates play); plays ONCE for the real board via a `boardSettled` mount gate (the store's populated placeholder was rendering a first, relocated entrance). Departs from ADR-0043 §B (a fall ACCELERATES, not decelerates) — board-unit motion is game-world physics, outside the chrome token ladder (like the move-hop). Drop is the reduced-motion DEFAULT (OS `reduce` is a false-positive here per ADR-0043 §C); `:root.reduce-motion` is the explicit fade-in-place opt-in. Landing sound + per-tile effect (ADR-0040) hook at the marked impact; deferred | accepted | 2026-06-29 |
| [0046](0046-screen-and-control-transitions-are-orchestrated.md) | Screen & control transitions are one ORCHESTRATED system (behaviour layer on ADR-0043 tokens): one built-in primitive `useScreenEntrance()` applied to a screen's CHROME ROOT (not a route wrapper) so the ambience backdrop stays continuous across art-background screens (rain never re-fades) AND no DOM/layout restructure; ONE fade speed everywhere (`--ds-duration-fade`) — entrance and in-panel crossfade are the identical fade, not surface-tiered; buffered choreography (fired by one explicit trigger, never reactively per asset-load — the cold-load reveal is the named violation, mitigated in follow-up; `--ds-stagger` between sequenced items; no overlap); inert during motion (trigger surfaces `pointer-events:none` until settle, mid-transition click queues as last); reduced-motion ungated for opacity; route-veil stays the heavy-route mask. Persistent-backdrop continuity is the hard constraint | superseded by [ADR-0205](0205-navigation-loads-atomic-scenes-through-one-director.md) | 2026-06-29 |
| [0047](0047-landing-sfx-are-authored-recordings.md) | Landing/terrain SFX are AUTHORED recordings, not code-synthesized: the rejected procedural `RECIPES` path stays deleted; runtime takes remain randomly selected without immediate repetition; and the terrain→sound assignment remains owner-editable. ADR-0085 supersedes this ADR's former Git source/runtime paths; ADR-0089 supersedes its committed configuration and localStorage-copy-to-source workflow with the live SFX profile | accepted; storage/config clauses superseded by ADR-0085 and ADR-0089 | 2026-06-29 |
| [0048](0048-water-tops-are-baked-frame-sheets.md) | Animated tile tops are baked frame sheets: water variants ship generated, alpha-locked 8-frame top sheets; code only advances frame index with per-cell phase while sides stay static. Opt-in via `topAnimFrames`; reduced-motion freezes only for the explicit in-game setting | accepted | 2026-07-01 |
| [0049](0049-route-surfaces-own-navigation-transitions.md) | Route surfaces own navigation transitions: a pure `routeSurfaces.ts` registry classifies routes as `heavy-board`, `heavy-editor`, `light-art`, or `light-plain`. Heavy board routes use veil + board-art gate; light art routes keep the shared menu backdrop/rain continuous and must fade only their chrome root through `ArtRouteChrome`/`LightArtRouteShell`; screens may not import `useScreenEntrance()` directly. `/skirmish` is explicitly light-art; `/play` is the board-art route | superseded by [ADR-0205](0205-navigation-loads-atomic-scenes-through-one-director.md) | 2026-07-02 |
| [0064](0064-victory-conditions-two-list-model.md) | Victory conditions become an if-then RULE/event model: optional `Level.victory = VictoryRule[]`, each `{ name?, if: VictoryCondition[] (ANDed), do: VictoryAction[] }` — win/lose are ACTIONS tagged to a faction (`ruleOutcome`: win(side)→side, lose(side)→other), and `do` is an array so spawn/gate/phase effects join later. `evaluateVictory` walks rules top-to-bottom, FIRST match decides (precedence = rule order; presets seed lose-above-win = defeat-first, so Survive's clock-hits-N-on-a-wipe = loss). Conditions eliminate/reach(PAWN-only, reads `lastMove`)/turnLimit; no per-unit tags. Save gate P6 is PER-FACTION (every on-board faction must be able to win AND lose). Authored in a full-size events OVERLAY over the board (panel.png frame; master-detail named-event list + detail; template dropdown that RESETS the victory rules; "Victory rules" / "Other events" tabs). Validated `validateLevel` + backend `validateWorkspaceVictory`. Renumbered from 0055 (collided twice with concurrent ADRs) | accepted; Events presentation clause superseded by ADR-0144 | 2026-07-03 |
| [0058](0058-every-route-is-click-reachable.md) | A dev surface is added to the Studio as a `catalogCategories` entry (grid + controls, plus a Viewer kind if it's an operated tool — the Game Lab is the `gamelab` kind, GameLabCatalog + GameLabViewer), reachable by its tab BY CONSTRUCTION per `studio-control-architecture.md` §"Adding to the studio" — never a standalone route with its own layout ("if adding it requires a new layout, the architecture is wrong"). No URL-only routes; wiring the click-path is part of building the surface and is verified by clicking to it — never deferred, never surfaced to the owner as a "where should this go?" question. Triggered by the Game Lab shipping as a URL-only bespoke route. Studio itself stays URL/bookmark-reached by design (ADR-0006 dev tier) | accepted; Viewer rail navigation refined by [0342](0342-studio-viewers-are-entered-through-catalog-and-own-only-local-controls.md) | 2026-07-03 |
| [0057](0057-studio-tuning-surfaces-reset-to-authoritative-baseline.md) | Every Studio tuning surface ships a Reset-to-AUTHORITATIVE-baseline control, wired before it ships: Reset restores the values the surface publishes from its real source of truth (live storage for live-content tuners, committed configuration for code-backed tuners), never a hand-copied "defaults" literal that rots. Untouched-then-reset is production-identical. Zero-out is not reset. A tuner without Reset is incomplete; it is never deferred or re-requested by the owner | accepted | 2026-07-03 |
| [0056](0056-vertical-rhythm-spacing-encodes-grouping.md) | Vertical spacing is a MONOTONE grouping ladder on ADR-0031 tokens: tight pair `--ds-inline-tight` (4) < within-section rows `--ds-stack` (8) < between-section `--ds-gutter` (12); headings bind DOWNWARD (gutter above, stack below — never closer to the previous group); hand-picked px on the rhythm axis banned; a declared `gap` must be live (block-container `gap` is dead CSS — the status card shipped one). Applied to the shared skirmish/level-editor rail family; other screens as touched | accepted | 2026-07-02 |
| [0055](0055-drawn-text-boxes-inset-content-by-the-role-token.md) | Any element that PAINTS a surface/outline around text insets that content symmetrically with `padding: var(--ds-inset)` (single-line inputs: `0 var(--ds-inset)` + min-height centering) — text never sits flush against a drawn edge; frameless sections stay zero-padding (container frame owns the inset, no double-pad); sprite/icon tiles exempt; density via ADR-0031 screen tokens, never per-element px. Applied to the level-editor rail (violations list had shipped flush); other screens migrate as touched | accepted | 2026-07-02 |
| [0054](0054-nine-slice-editor-is-the-devs-calibration-bench.md) | The 9-slice editor is the dev's calibration bench; shared family geometry and per-member calibration remain owner-operable | accepted; Git-owned config/bake-path clauses superseded by ADR-0106 | 2026-07-03 |
| [0053](0053-battle-clock-time-control.md) | Battle clock: optional per-level chess time control (`Level.timeControl`, whole seconds) for the PLAYER only — Fischer increment banked per completed move, deadline-based countdown that runs only on the player's live turn, flag fall = defeat; authored in the RULES panel (ladder steppers), displayed as a center title-bar chip between turn plate and objective; validated structurally + P5_TIME_CONTROL | accepted; untimed-display clause partially superseded by [0451](0451-untimed-levels-display-elapsed-battle-time.md) | 2026-07-03 |
| [0052](0052-game-controls-are-buttons-routes-are-addresses.md) | Game controls are BUTTONS, not hyperlinks: every player-facing nav control converts to the shared `NavButton` (`<button>` → `navigateApp`, hover/focus warm via the relocated `routePrefetch.ts`, thunk `to` retires the gear's stale-href rewrite hack, native `disabled` replaces aria-disabled fakes); routes stay pure ADDRESSES (deep links/reload/back-forward unchanged). Link affordances (status-bar URL, link context menu, middle-click new-tab, drag ghost) are deliberately shed — do not re-emulate. Deliberate anchors: brand lockup, auth sign-ins, external links, download blobs | accepted | 2026-07-02 |
| [0051](0051-light-hop-exit-dissolve-and-readiness-held-entrances.md) | Light-art hops dissolve the OUTGOING chrome (`.screen-exit`, `--route-exit-ms` 200ms — route-choreography family, shorter than the entrance on purpose) before the swap, gated by `routeScreenKey` so same-screen sub-navigation (settings tabs, campaign rail) never blinks; entrances gain a readiness hold (`ArtRouteChrome ready` prop → hold→fade→settled, 4s failsafe) so async screens reveal REAL content instead of an empty frame + pop (ADR-0046 C.1 realized; Campaign is the founding consumer); hover/focus intent also warms route DATA (campaign hydration), so the hold is ~0 in practice | superseded by [ADR-0205](0205-navigation-loads-atomic-scenes-through-one-director.md) | 2026-07-02 |
| [0050](0050-game-modes-placement-and-playability.md) | Game modes = the per-level win rule (`ObjectiveType`, stored ids unchanged — owner names are labels: Last Man Standing/King Assault/Rival Kings/Survive/Reach; `rival-kings` NEW; `capture-king` becomes direction-aware via `kingSide`) + an orthogonal `placement: fixed\|random` toggle (random = roster counts per side, NO painted units, seeded placement into the existing `player-spawn`/`enemy-spawn` zones — first-ever consumer). Board floor drops 4×4→1×1; the real gate is NEW `validatePlayability` (presence/king/zone-capacity/surviveTurns rules): the EDITOR blocks Save per level with plain-language violations; the backend stays structural (whole-workspace PUT must not be bricked by one legacy level). Editor saves author real zones (the `zones: []` hard-code + wipe dies); `surviveTurns` becomes a schema field. First gameplay-rules ADR; builds on ADR-0038 | accepted; explicit side-specific deployment authoring partially superseded by [0287](0287-deployment-is-a-dedicated-side-specific-authoring-workspace.md) | 2026-07-01 |
| [0060](0060-playing-never-requires-sign-in.md) | Playing never requires sign-in — reads are public, only writes are login-gated; anonymous play + share-link unfurls preserved, a login wall rejected; sets the read-public/write-gated rule that all DB-backed tweakable content (props next) inherits; generalizes ADR-0038 | accepted | 2026-07-03 |
| [0061](0061-prop-seats-are-db-tuned-live-content.md) | Prop seats remain live-tunable through the global `prop_seats/default` row (public GET / `requireAdmin` PUT), `/prop-lab` still saves the whole document instantly, and frontend/server rendering share it. ADR-0085 supersedes the committed baseline, overlay, last-good fallback, and DB→Git bake-back: the row is now complete and availability-critical, and rendering fails closed when it is missing or invalid | accepted; production render-scale publication superseded by [0076](0076-scaling-is-calibration-production-art-is-native-1x.md); storage/fallback clauses superseded by [0085](0085-runtime-assets-are-live-storage-backed.md) | 2026-07-03 |
| [0063](0063-section-dividers-are-a-1d-bar-primitive-teeing-into-the-rail.md) | Section dividers are a 1-D horizontal "bar" kit primitive (`panel-divider`, registry `kind: "bar"`; distinct from the existing decorative settings `section-divider-frame`), rendered as N children in a panel's content flow for arbitrary count/position. `border-image` can't hold an interior bar (ADR-0002's fixed 3×3), so a divider is its own layered element — NOT baked into a taller panel. Establishes a rail-JUNCTION family derived from the `corner` atom by mirroring it into each concave angle: `corner`=2-way (exists), `tee`=3-way (`composeTee` = corner+flipV, `kind:"junction"`), `cross`=4-way (`composeCross`, banked for grids). The divider (`buildBarFromTee`) seats an AUTHORED 3-way tee atom (`corner-t` — the frame's gold corner made symmetric with the corner flair removed) at each end, mirrored, teeing into the side rail as the frame's own corner, just branched; drawn at native 24px and rendered 1:1 = crisp. (Revised from the initial `composeTee`=corner+flipV derivation, which reproduced the corner flair at a flair-less junction and broke under rescale; `composeTee`/`composeCross` remain for the standalone derived `tee`/`cross` glyphs — ADR-0012's mirror-a-corner, kept for those.) One registry entry, parity- + tee-geometry-tested; excluded from the 4-corner editor/catalog-edit (no per-corner DOF). Consumed as `.kit-divider` (a horizontal 1-D border-image, the 90°-rotate of the scrollbar slice) placed by ONE rule: bleed out by the host's `--frame-w` so the tee reaches the rail on any frame width. Reuses ADR-0016 + ADR-0059; horizontal-only scope is extended by ADR-0239 | accepted; horizontal-only scope partially superseded by [0239](0239-run-army-rows-use-a-divided-portrait-bay-and-explicit-type-hierarchy.md) | 2026-07-04 |
| [0062](0062-settings-twin-rail-is-placed-by-one-shared-rule.md) | The settings-twin rail (home menu · Settings · Campaign, one shared `.settings-shell`/`.settings-rail-frame` family) is placed by ONE shared rule — no per-surface offset. PR #339's home-ONLY margin fork (`.main-menu-home .settings-shell`, floored 270 vs the siblings' unfloored) desynced the home rail 10–190px right of the Settings rows it must align with below ~1780px vw ("too indented"). Fix: move the anti-clip floor onto the shared `.settings-shell` margin (floored at 230 = `MM_LIVE.btnX`, "the rail never crosses x=0"), delete the fork + `main-menu-home` class; home/Settings/Campaign now byte-identical at every width (verified 0px delta, 1280–1920). A `mmLive.test` assertion bans any per-surface `.settings-shell` margin override. Knock-on fixed in the same change: the shared floor would push the fixed-width 931px rows panel off-screen right below ~1410px vw (pre-existing broken band — the rail clipped there before), so the panel gained a responsive `max-width` ceiling (~20px gutter below ~1410px, full 931px above — owner's ≥1680px widths untouched; rail placement unaffected). Instance of ADR-0059 | accepted | 2026-07-04 |
| [0063](0063-rail-tab-continuity-is-data-indexed-not-dom-positioned.md) | Rail-tab stone CONTINUITY (the sibling axis to ADR-0062's placement, same `.settings-tab` control) is keyed to a data-owned `--tab-index` each renderer sets from its map position, NOT a `:nth-child` ladder: the shared skin reads as one continuous texture sheet on every rail (main menu 5 tabs, Settings 4, Campaign N + interleaved group dividers) via `background-position-y: calc(var(--tab-index) * -98px)`. Fixes the seam the main menu's trailing Settings tab showed (an uncovered 5th child restarting the stone at 0, from #282); nth-child was wrong in kind — coupled to rail length AND sibling structure (Campaign's dividers already mis-counted). Guarded by `settingsRailContinuity.test.ts` (fails if the ladder returns or a `.settings-tab` renderer omits the index). Second instance of the ADR-0062 rule: a reported UI inconsistency is fixed at the shared source of truth + guard, never a one-off patch where it showed | accepted | 2026-07-04 |
| [0064](0064-homepage-backdrop-is-one-continuous-instance.md) | The homepage backdrop (animated menu scene + synced rain) is ONE continuous instance owned by `HomepageBackdrop`, re-parented into whichever homepage-family screen mounts it — closing the ADR-0046 §G/0049 gap where only the rain was continuous and each screen re-mounted its own `SceneBackdrop` (the menu→editor "re-adjust": a fresh per-screen scene recomputes its cover-crop). The scene DOM is built once by a shared `buildSceneBackdropNode`; the `SceneBackdrop` React component is kept only for the studio inspector (SceneAnimLab). The level editor joins the family (renders the shared backdrop as a sibling OUTSIDE its faded chrome, drops its `::before` battlefield), while `/play` keeps the battlefield world. Guarded by `check-light-art-chrome.mjs` (single-owner: no bespoke AmbienceBackground; no SceneBackdrop component in a homepage screen). Structural lock per ADR-0059 | accepted | 2026-07-04 |
## Minor decisions (no ADR)
Small, low-risk calls not worth a full record:
- **2026-06-26 — Drop the Settings content-pane heading.** Removed the visible
  "SETTINGS / General" heading: the brand lockup already shows the screen and the
  active nav button shows the section, and the concept has no such heading. Kept a
  visually-hidden `h2` for screen-reader structure.
- **2026-07-02 — Settings gets a title-bar "‹ Back" (return-to-origin).** Going to
  Settings from any surface was a one-way street — only the brand logo (→ home) or the
  browser Back. Fix: the account-cluster gear records the current location as
  `?returnTo=<origin>`; Settings renders a "‹ Back" to it, validated same-origin via
  `readValidatedReturnTo` in `ui/navigation.ts` (resolves through the URL parser so
  host-escape tricks like `/\host` or tab/CR-injected hosts are rejected — string prefix
  checks aren't enough) and threaded through its own tab/tracks links so the origin
  survives every in-Settings hop. Gear href refreshed on pointerdown AND keydown-Enter
  (screens rewrite their query via `replaceState` without re-rendering the persistent
  bar; keyboard fires no pointer event). Placement was researched against Apple HIG /
  Material / GNOME / NN-g — which favor a *leading*-edge back — but we chose the
  **trailing actions slot** instead: this app's leading edge is the fixed brand anchor
  (must never move) and its trailing cluster is already the navigation/system home
  (gear · mute · account), so the Level Editor's existing `‹ Back`/`‹ Catalog` and this
  one now share one consistent spot. Fits ADR-0042's actions slot (which already lists
  `‹ Catalog`) with no invariant change.
| [0059](0059-reuse-the-canonical-primitive-not-a-bespoke-parallel.md) | Reuse the canonical shared primitive; a bespoke PARALLEL reimplementation of something the repo already does one blessed way is a defect (ADR-0059 violation). Search before you build; if a primitive is genuinely missing, make it shared + registered, not inlined. Ships a findable INDEX of the canonical primitives — ViewPane (board viewport pan/zoom), BoardLabBoard/TileGrid (board render), PropSprite/BoardStructure (props/doodads seating), .board-unit-seat (unit seat), the Studio catalogCategories/ViewerKind shell, SliderRow/ctlReset (controls), the nine-slice kit, AppTitleBar, NavButton. The general rule the per-domain ADRs (0058/0032/0057/0042/0016) are each an instance of | accepted | 2026-07-03 |
| [0065](0065-editor-is-a-settings-twin-at-editor-route.md) | The Editor becomes a settings-twin at the canonical route `/editor` (was `/campaigns-next`), with the level editor nested at `/editor/level` (was `/edit` / `/level-editor`) — routes now name what the user sees; legacy paths kept as aliases. It drops its bespoke `.ce-*` three-panel + footer chrome for the shared menu/settings shell (rail of campaign tabs + pinned workspace-verb footer, one scrolling content column with the live preview pinned on top), a fourth member of the menu · Settings · Campaign family mirroring `Campaign.tsx`; supersedes the campaign-editor art contract's bespoke-chrome direction (reused surfaces are real 9-slice kit, so "no CSS imitation" still holds). `SettingsSection`/`Row`/`Button` extracted to `ui/shared/SettingsControls.tsx` and shared with Settings. All behavior preserved. Not in conflict with ADR-0033 (the level editor keeps its board+control-panel shape; only its route moved). Instance of ADR-0059, sibling of ADR-0062 | accepted | 2026-07-04 |
| [0066](0066-title-bar-rule-is-a-forged-tileset.md) | The title-bar rule is a forged TILESET, extending ADR-0037's bottom rule into a run+joint system. Splits the RIVET (one shared atom, placed UPRIGHT in every piece so rivets never drift between orientations) from the STRAP (two orientation-lit atoms — horizontal LEDGE with a bright top edge vs symmetric vertical WALL — because a top-down light is orientation-dependent and rotating a strip side-lights it, the recurring bug); `compose-titlebar-runs.py` composites the shared rivet onto each strap into the run tiles `band-forged` (E-W, repeat-x) and `rail-forged` (N-S, repeat-y). Two DISTINCT joints: a SQUARE intersection cover (an overlay that BLOCKS the crossing — robust across widths since the band's rivet phase under the wall isn't fixed, so a merged ⊥ can't stay aligned) and a DIAMOND centre cap (also the asset ADR-0037's interactive Retry stud overlays). All codex-GENERATED atoms (method-verified, ADR-0011/0013/0014), atom-assembled (ADR-0012), never code-drawn; a forge symmetry gate rejects side-lit strap rolls. The wall renders on the invariant cluster (ADR-0042) so the rule is on every screen, full-bleed to the top edge as the band is to the bottom. Retires `band-studded` (app use), `ornament-nailstud`, and the dead exploratory joints; `check-titlebar-rule-kit.mjs` blocks their return | accepted | 2026-07-04 |
| [0067](0067-board-previews-render-on-the-level-background-not-a-checkerboard.md) | Board previews render on the level's world background rather than a Studio transparency checker. Its dense-solid baked-crop implementation is superseded by ADR-0189's canonical live and static board-relative framing. | accepted; dense-solid crop and static-only clauses partially superseded by [0189](0189-board-facing-views-open-on-playable-geometry.md) | 2026-07-04 |
| [0068](0068-link-copy-controls-are-side-effect-free.md) | Link-copy controls are side-effect free: the Level Editor Board panel's current "Share Link" button, and any replacement, must only copy the current browser address bar URL; it must not save, publish, create/update `public_maps`, call `/api/maps/publish`, mutate app state, rewrite the URL, or navigate. Any publishing workflow must be a separate explicitly labeled action | accepted | 2026-07-08 |
| [0069](0069-board-solver-is-the-front-of-the-per-board-ai-pipeline.md) | Board SOLVING is the front of the per-board AI pipeline: given any board, estimate feasibility up front (state-space + memory + verdict — the computed toy-vs-chess answer), then run a BOUNDED, ANYTIME solve — retrograde strong-solve (full tablebase + honest piece values by ablation, win-distance/DTM handling for the loopy no-repetition game) when small enough, iterative-deepening αβ weak-solve (TT + cycle draws, GHI-safe) when not. Two faces over one pure engine (core/solver): an in-Studio interactive stepper (bender-world/eight-queens idiom — drive it, watch it think phase by phase) and a cluster solve-runs surface cloned from train-runs. Written as provisional 0068, renumbered 0069 after main's 0068 (link-copy) merged first | accepted | 2026-07-05 |
| [0070](0070-board-content-answers-to-the-one-content-system.md) | Board content answers to the ONE content system: no compiled-in Level objects behind UI (a shadow content system is a defect — the owner must always be able to answer "where does it live / how do I reach it / how do I change it"); teaching/demo boards are authored DEMO-TIER levels (ADR-0038 tiering ⇒ scenario/unfiltered/demo filter falls out); a viewer opens ON a selected item — item-less viewer states filled with fallback content must not exist (candidate selection is the catalog's job); deep links reach only states clicking can produce (extends ADR-0058 routes → STATES); backend failure stays loud — no compiled-in data may keep a surface looking healthy when its source is down. Ships in named violation: the Board Solver demoBoards.ts + rail select + item-less state; rework pending, owner-directed. Trigger: the solver demo-board incident, 2026-07-08 | accepted | 2026-07-08 |
| [0071](0071-the-deliverable-is-the-instrument.md) | The deliverable is the INSTRUMENT, not the outcome — the agent builds the meta-project (the project around the project): tools that put assembly, inspection, judgment, tuning and audit in the OWNER's hands, because he is the domain expert (40 years of games, the feel of game AI) and the agent is weak at exactly what makes the game good (visual alignment, feel, adapting canon to this game). Rules: a feature is done when HE can drive it, not when it works; owner-operable end-to-end (clickable, owner-governed content, knobs on the surface, legible state); judgment routes to the owner through pickers/steppers/before-afters — never baked in as fait accompli; canonical processes (Stockfish et al.) adapted as instruments under his hands, not recited pipelines; shortcuts are NAMED DEBT against this ADR, never silent "done". The general rule behind 0054/0057/0058/0070 and the Studio/Gym/editor family; reference feel: the owner's bender-world and eight-queens steppers. Ships with named debt: the solver stepper's scrub-a-recording semantics, hardcoded bounds, missing cluster trace | accepted | 2026-07-08 |
| [0072](0072-castling-and-chess-draw-rules-as-authored-events.md) | Castling and the chess draw rules (50-move, threefold) are AUTHORED per-level events on the "Other events" tab, completing chess parity for the AI-training control group (en passant / underpromotion / stalemate already existed). Castling: one `castle` event per king-rook pair with explicit squares (the Castling template scans the board and bakes chess geometry); play-time legality is chess-exact off a new history-exact `Piece.hasMoved`; encoded as ONE king move to a unique destination carrying `castle:{rookId,rookTo}`, so click/drag, premoves, the netplay relay, replay, and search all work unchanged. Draws: `chess-draws` event → `GameState.drawRules`; `halfmoveClock` lives in `applyMove` (search-visible for free); the threefold `positionCounts` table is maintained at COMMITTED-move sites only (`recordPosition`; never in applyMove — 200k search nodes) and restarts on every irreversible move; `positionKey` is FIDE-exact (placement + turn + castle rights + legal-en-passant); `ruleDraw` is mate-precedence-exact; `negamax` adds search-path repetition. Backend workspace validation mirrors the new kinds. Event-free levels are byte-identical to before | accepted | 2026-07-08 |
| [0073](0073-unit-art-is-live-storage-backed-content.md) | Board-unit art is live content behind six stable gameplay ids (`pawn`...`king`): Postgres owns accepted pointers, display scale, geometry, candidate lifecycle and audit metadata; private Blob Storage owns content-addressed PNG bytes; the backend serves immutable same-origin sprite URLs; one required shared registry drives browser gameplay/editor rendering and server thumbnails. Candidate UUIDs and former art-derived ids never enter level data. Accepting complete 6-palette x 8-direction art atomically swaps the family pointer and archives the old art. Git-backed board sprites and their generators are deleted; portraits and rocks stay separate | accepted; production display-scale clause superseded by [0076](0076-scaling-is-calibration-production-art-is-native-1x.md) | 2026-07-09 |
| [0074](0074-one-play-entry-one-shared-selector.md) | One top-level Play entry opens one shared light-art selector: pinned Skirmish + Levels above a scrolling Campaign collection; exact `/play` stays the live board, old `/skirmish` + `/campaign[/<id>]` pickers are retired, and missing content never synthesizes a client-side board | accepted | 2026-07-10 |
| [0075](0075-unit-directions-are-blender-authored.md) | New unit geometry and eight-way orientation are Blender-authored; a size-only revision instead recaptures the complete accepted 6-palette x 8-direction asset with aspect-preserving, premultiplied-alpha area reduction into the Unit Studio delivery canvas. Provenance must disclose source, contained, and delivery rasters plus spatial resampling; image generation, sheet restyling, independently invented facings, and nonuniform stretching are ineligible | accepted; production recapture acceptance clause superseded by [0076](0076-scaling-is-calibration-production-art-is-native-1x.md) | 2026-07-10 |
| [0076](0076-scaling-is-calibration-production-art-is-native-1x.md) | Scaling is a CALIBRATION instrument, never production acceptance: tune a candidate at any scale to settle its frame, visible footprint and anchor, then regenerate/re-render/re-forge at those required pixels. Accepted raster art contains no spatial resampling step to reach its target and renders source pixel → app logical pixel 1:1 at canonical 100%; crop/pad/chroma/mask/atlas assembly without resampling remain allowed, while camera/user zoom, DPR variants and declared 9-slice/tile regions are compositor exceptions. A scaled candidate is an explicit bridge until native regeneration + dimension/provenance guard + live 1× proof pass. Refines ADR-0040 and supersedes prior clean/live-downscale allowances in 0026/0027/0061/0073 plus ADR-0075's production recapture acceptance clause | accepted; whole-board exception governed by [0158](0158-immutable-predrawn-background-versions-own-derived-raster-and-occlusion.md), eight exact Run lipsanon outputs by [0332](0332-eight-run-lipsanon-icons-ship-the-approved-resized-pixels.md) | 2026-07-10 |
| [0077](0077-multiplayer-is-one-game-projected-through-seat-local-clients.md) | Multiplayer is one canonical `(level, seed, ordered moves)` simulation projected through one seat-local interface per participant: `player`/`enemy` stay serialized faction ids while all "you/opponent" copy, controls, overlays, objectives, results and premoves derive from `localSide`; the server sequences one pending move intent and clients commit only ordered echo/backfill; every async effect is scoped to a match generation; `applyMove` owns mechanics while one initial-and-post-commit adjudicator supplies authored-rule/checkmate/draw outcomes to solo, lobby, AI, self-play and search. Live Leave is resignation; observers are rejected from interactive play until a spectator UI exists; timed levels cannot silently run untimed | accepted; relay selection-retention clause partially superseded by [0188](0188-committed-movers-release-client-selection.md) | 2026-07-11 |
| [0077](0077-wall-mirrors-are-live-piece-reflective-surfaces.md) | Every wall-decor source whose `kind` is `mirror` is a LIVE piece-reflective surface backed by one canonical fixed-camera 2D reflection planner, with no decorative or off mode: piece centres reflect across the physical perimeter wall plane (west at `x = -0.5` sends `(x, y)` to `(-1 - x, y)`, north at `y = -0.5` sends it to `(x, -1 - y)`) before passing through the canonical board projection, and the planner consumes the same live or authored physical-piece snapshot as its host board, so movement, capture and authored setup changes are visible in the glass. A convex mirror is a deterministic aperture-local lens treatment applied over that same truthful plan — not a prerecorded scene and not a second reflection engine — the aperture belongs to the frame asset, and a multi-wall mirror composes as one continuous lens rather than repeated one-tile pictures | superseded by ADR-0078 | 2026-07-11 |
| [0078](0078-lobby-authority-survives-retry-reconnect-and-departure.md) | Lobby authority survives transport races: every gesture has a stable idempotent intent id; Start is ready-only; deterministic results publish only after matching reports from both seats; multiplayer eligibility comes from canonical server content; and a departed host leaves a reconnectable move/result tombstone until both seats acknowledge it or TTL expires | accepted | 2026-07-11 |
| [0078](0078-wall-mirrors-reflect-pieces-horizontally-in-screen-space.md) | Reflect an already-projected physical piece horizontally about the vertical centreline of its aperture — `reflectedX = 2 × apertureCenterX − projectedPieceX` with projected Y copied unchanged — and draw the piece's own directional sprite under a horizontal flip. This is an intentional isometric game convention and explicitly not an approximation of wall-plane or perspective ray geometry: north and west use the identical screen-space equation, so the wall face decides only where the asset and its aperture are seated and the planner never reflects board coordinates across `x = -0.5` or `y = -0.5`. Aperture clipping, lens treatment, multi-wall continuity and renderer parity from ADR-0077 remain intact | superseded by ADR-0079 | 2026-07-11 |
| [0079](0079-lobby-recovery-pins-content-and-unresolved-intent.md) | Lobby recovery pins both kinds of identity across reload: the client durably journals an unresolved move before sending and retries that id after backfill, while Start deep-clones/fingerprints canonical level content for every replay. The first result report freezes the prefix; any live participant departure makes a discoverable seat-owned tombstone until acknowledgement/TTL | accepted | 2026-07-11 |
| [0079](0079-wall-mirrors-use-wall-local-board-axis-visibility-corridors.md) | Each mirror casts a half-infinite visibility corridor inward from the authored wall cells it covers — a west mirror along board `+X` filtering by continuous board Y, a north mirror along `+Y` filtering by continuous board X — and only physical pieces inside it are submitted to the reflection. The authored wall-cell span defines the tangent interval `I(a, N) = [a − 0.5, a + N − 0.5)`, half-open so adjacent spans can never both admit the same piece and boundary ownership stays deterministic; the corridor comes from those supporting wall cells, never from screen-space aperture bounds, frame pixels or the field-of-view setting. In-flight movement is admitted on the piece's exact continuous coordinate rather than a rounded source or destination cell, and a multi-span mirror uses its full authored coverage as ONE corridor and one composition instead of repeating eligibility per wall tile | superseded by ADR-0080 | 2026-07-11 |
| [0080](0080-lobby-ambiguity-fails-closed-and-stays-resolvable.md) | Lobby ambiguity fails closed but remains owner-resolvable: one browser tab holds the interactive `(lobby,seat)` Web Lock and journals only to cross-tab durable storage, while terminal-report disagreement becomes a frozen visible `result_disputed` state with a confirmed concession/abandon path instead of resumed play or a TTL-only dead end | accepted | 2026-07-11 |
| [0080](0080-wall-mirrors-reflect-along-the-board-grid-wall-normal.md) | After corridor admission, reflect the piece's continuous BOARD-GRID coordinate across its supporting perimeter wall — holding the wall-tangent grid coordinate fixed and applying field of view only to wall-normal grid depth — and then pass that reflected coordinate through the canonical orthographic-isometric projection, replacing ADR-0078's screen-space horizontal reflection. The physical piece, its wall-plane intersection and its reflected anchor therefore stay collinear along the relevant in-game grid axis at every field-of-view value, and that axis is expected to appear diagonal on screen. Depth is compressed about the wall plane BEFORE projection rather than about a screen-space focal point afterward, with no perspective mirror camera, shear, nonuniform foreshortening or second projection path; ADR-0079's corridor intervals, boundary ownership and multi-span union are unchanged | superseded by ADR-0081 | 2026-07-11 |
| [0081](0081-empty-control-panel-frames-are-overlays-not-layout-borders.md) | Empty outer control-panel frames are overlays, not layout borders: transparent shells draw ornament as an absolute overlay while fill color comes only from explicit fill layers. Missing fill must stay visibly transparent, and the level editor guard blocks fake padding/moat regressions | accepted | 2026-07-06 |
| [0081](0081-wall-mirrors-reflect-piece-facing-in-board-grid-space.md) | Reflect the physical piece's FACING vector in board-grid space per wall — `west: (vx, vy) → (−vx, vy)`, `north: (vx, vy) → (vx, −vy)` — and then select the accepted directional sprite whose horizontally flipped raster displays that reflected facing, so orientation and position finally use the same wall normal while the raster flip keeps supplying mirror chirality. Facing is semantic board state read from the piece, never parsed out of a sprite URL or inferred from raster pixels, which means the shared reflection subject must carry unit identity, palette and facing rather than an opaque already-selected raster; north and west mirrors may therefore choose different sprites for the same physical piece, and the accepted eight-direction catalog remains the only source of directional art — no mirror camera and no second reverse-side sprite set | superseded by ADR-0082 | 2026-07-11 |
| [0081](0081-td-runs-are-a-per-level-experiment-library.md) | `BooksBlob.tdRuns` is a per-level run LIBRARY rather than one mutable session: a `TdRunDoc` is the old autosave payload wrapped with `id`, `name` and `createdAt`, `activeId` names the run the pane has open, and a fresh pane is unattached so its FIRST autosave allocates a new run; ids never rewind, so deleting the last run keeps `nextId`. Settings are frozen per run because the ε/α schedules anneal over a fixed budget and restoring a session without its exact opts would corrupt it — changing a knob therefore means **+ New run**, which shelves the open run and unfreezes a fresh pane seeded with the same knobs — and **Delete run** is the only discard, with the transport's `↺ reset` retired so open/rename/new/compare never destroy anything. Compare is a first-class view: one column per run, with the settings rows that DIFFER highlighted because those are the experiment, and outcomes beside pawn = 1 learned values. Adoption records carry provenance and survive deletion — the live record sits at blob level (`BooksBlob.tdAdoption`, shown "(deleted)") and is validated by exact vector match, while each run keeps its own copy as history — and `migrateTdRuns` folds the retired `tdSession` field in as `Run 1` on load, hoisting its adoption record, and never writes that field again | accepted | 2026-07-11 |
| [0082](0082-control-panel-chrome-has-outer-and-inner-roles.md) | Control-panel house chrome has exactly two box roles, `outer` and `inner`; components select a role instead of inventing rail/atom/frame dimensions locally. Dividers remain child primitives rather than a third box role; ADR-0092 replaces their outer-only inheritance with host-role inheritance | accepted; outer-only divider inheritance superseded by ADR-0092; Events outer-panel clause superseded by ADR-0144 | 2026-07-06 |
| [0082](0082-wall-mirrors-are-exact-one-to-one-game-world-reflections.md) | Every wall mirror is an EXACT one-to-one game-world reflection: position is the complete board-grid wall-plane transform (`west: r = (−1 − px, py)`, `north: r = (px, −1 − py)`) with no field-of-view factor, grid-depth compression, aperture-centre fit or post-projection focal convergence, and the reflected draw uses the physical board draw's own resolved screen width and height with no independent subject-scale multiplier — so a reflected piece is the same size as its board counterpart and its floor contact is exact, fractional in-flight coordinates included. Glass may still alter material appearance or opacity but never reflected position, size or contact; the mirror-bearing wall and aperture supply the headroom a taller presentation needs without changing the wall's logical plane, footprint or covered cells; and Studio exposes and PROVES the fixed rule instead of offering controls that could violate it | superseded by ADR-0083 | 2026-07-11 |
| [0082](0082-the-levels-ai-is-a-named-approach.md) | A level's AI is a NAMED approach with its own parameter set, not a loose vector: a registry (`game/aiApproach.ts`) names each one — today only `material-search`, displayed as **Tuned material search** with the technique line "alpha-beta search over a material evaluation — unit values learned by afterstate TD(λ) self-play (Beal & Smith) or SPSA champion tuning" — and BOTH tuning surfaces (the Piece-values pane's TD runs and the Training tab's SPSA champions) tune that one approach's vector, so the surface is provenance rather than a different approach. `BooksBlob.levelAi = { live?, approaches: { [id]: { vector, adoption? } } }` is the level-AI document: `live` names the approach in force (absent = stock AI) and each approach owns its own config, so repointing `live` never destroys a sibling's tuning; `setLevelAiApproach` adopts the whole pointer — approach, parameters and provenance together — while `clearLevelAiApproach` drops one config and `sanitizeLevelAi` guards the load boundary. The resolver is unchanged: `game/adoptedWeights` still caches the live vector per level for the synchronous personal → shipped → default read before every enemy reply. The audit box names the recipe, and still names the engine when nothing is set ("material search (stock)"), because the approach concept is true of the stock tier too; `migrateLevelAi` folds legacy `adoptedWeights` plus a vector-matching `tdAdoption` into a live material-search config on load | accepted | 2026-07-12 |
| [0083](0083-chrome-frame-geometry-is-derived-not-authored-state.md) | Chrome family state persists visible art-direction decisions only; invisible geometry is derived; ADR-0092 keys divider geometry by host role | accepted; outer-only divider clauses superseded by ADR-0092; Git-owned persistence clauses superseded by ADR-0106 | 2026-07-10 |
| [0083](0083-mirror-aperture-coverage-is-authored-per-asset.md) | Aperture coverage is an authored ASSET ROLE downstream of one invariant exact reflection: a small mirror may crop the unchanged 1:1 raster at its glass boundary, while Grand Gallery and any future full-body mirror must use a tall mirror-bearing wall, frame and aperture that contain the complete unit silhouette. Cropping is a MASK RESULT, not an alternate reflection transform — the live raster is clipped to the frame-owned glass polygon only after corridor admission, exact reflection, facing resolution and canonical projection, and the aperture never feeds back into subject position, scale or depth. An exact-size knight partially seen through a small oval is correct; a shrunken knight fitted inside it is not | superseded by ADR-0086 | 2026-07-11 |
| [0084](0084-accepted-chrome-rails-are-native-size-directional-families.md) | Accepted outer and inner rails are native-size directional families: generated at installed thickness, rendered 1:1 on the short axis, and admitted through no-resample gates. Repeat rails need seam proof; long rails crop only below covering atoms. ADR-0088 moves candidate publication out of Git, while ADR-0092 makes each divider inherit its host rail | accepted; Git-backed candidate storage superseded by ADR-0088 and outer-only divider inheritance superseded by ADR-0092 | 2026-07-10 |
| [0084](0084-full-body-mirrors-prove-grounded-board-axis-line-of-sight.md) | A full-body mirror must pass BOTH exact virtual-raster containment and an exhaustive grounded board-axis wall-crossing proof, with the live piece's physical draw treated as a board-anchored billboard that is never paired with a different directional sprite. Every opaque destination pixel is classified by reproducing the live draw's destination rectangle, `contain` fit and source alpha sampling — transparent source pixels do not participate — and each pixel's `wallHit` must land inside the complete authored aperture AND inside the union of that aperture's supporting-segment clips, so decorative overhang can never make an unsupported hit valid. A handful of representative rays may explain the geometry, but the verdict comes from the complete wall-hit silhouette | superseded by ADR-0085 | 2026-07-12 |
| [0085](0085-runtime-assets-are-live-storage-backed.md) | Runtime, review, candidate, and source-media binaries are live-storage-backed behind stable semantic slots: Postgres owns active pointers, accepted status, typed metadata, provenance, revisions and audit; private Blob owns immutable content-addressed bytes; the backend resolves stable `/assets/<slot>` and immutable hash routes for browser, CSS, Studio, gameplay and thumbnails. Git owns code/geometry/prompts, never accepted bytes or pointers; generation tools upload candidates; no committed fallback or filesystem reader remains. Generalizes ADR-0073 beyond units and supersedes storage-only clauses that kept UI, terrain, props, SFX, fonts and other media in `frontend/public` | accepted; mandatory test-slot cutover gate superseded by [0086](0086-runtime-asset-cutover-uses-one-live-data-plane.md), and semantic-slot-only identity plus public-read/admin-write access partially superseded by [0158](0158-immutable-predrawn-background-versions-own-derived-raster-and-occlusion.md) for owner-scoped exact pre-drawn versions | 2026-07-11 |
| [0085](0085-mirror-surfaces-end-at-the-wall-floor-boundary.md) | Every mirror layer is clipped to the union of its real supporting wall-face segments, whose lower edge is the generated wall's projected back-edge/floor seam, so the boundary tile owns the board-side half-plane — at the current projection `west: y = −28 − (27/48)x` and `north: y = −28 + (27/48)x`, with pixel centres on or above the line on the wall side and centres strictly below it absent from the mirror draw. Depth bands and support masks are distinct primitives: an ordinary coplanar wall-art depth band may stay vertically unbounded to partition painter order, but that is never evidence that glass or frame pixels have wall behind them. Generated frame, generated glass and the live reflection all share the one support union. A topology and occlusion rule downstream of the exact reflection transform — reflection geometry is unchanged | accepted; supersedes ADR-0084 | 2026-07-12 |
| [0086](0086-runtime-asset-cutover-uses-one-live-data-plane.md) | Runtime-asset cutover uses the one live app data plane: import and final-image verification run through unserved candidate pods against the existing Postgres/Blob authority; no production-seeded test database is a required release or owner-verification gate. Synthetic transient CI databases remain test-process details, not content environments or promotion authorities | accepted; partially supersedes ADR-0085's mandatory test-slot gate | 2026-07-12 |
| [0086](0086-all-perimeter-walls-use-full-height-geometry.md) | Every perimeter wall uses the FULL-HEIGHT generated wall geometry, retiring both the mirror-only tall wall variant and the ordinary short runtime wall lane. Wall material may vary; wall HEIGHT may not — not by mirror presence, wall-art kind or placement state — so wall-art manifests and placements carry no `wallVariant` or mirror-support-height flag and adding or removing Grand Gallery changes only wall art and live reflection state while the supporting wall raster stays the same canonical full-height wall. Mirror metadata describes aperture INTENT instead (`mirrorCoverage: "full-body"` for Grand Gallery, `"authored-crop"` for the small mirrors), a semantic role that governs proofs and never geometry, and Grand Gallery remains a full-body aperture uncropped by its authored glass | accepted; supersedes ADR-0083 | 2026-07-12 |
| [0087](0087-exposed-terrain-faces-own-independent-edge-treatments.md) | Exposed south/east terrain faces resolve independently from occupancy and select independent side sources through one shared browser/editor/thumbnail contract; Water uses an intentional native thin-cap abrupt cut while waterfalls remain explicit, and top seam repair stays inside the occupied footprint | accepted; partially supersedes ADR-0039/0041 whole-cell side composition | 2026-07-12 |
| [0087](0087-grand-gallery-wall-faces-are-exact-visual-counterparts.md) | Grand Gallery's west projected face is the canonical emitted material and its north face is the pixel-exact horizontal counterpart of it, for both the generated frame and the generated glass: north equals mirrored west at every RGBA sample, `mountX` becomes `width − west.mountX` with `mountY` unchanged, and the north normalized aperture is west's horizontal mirror with polygon winding restored so runtime convex clipping stays valid. Asset parity alone was not enough — Grand Gallery is depth-partitioned into one draw slice per supporting wall, and exact-abutting canvas clips let the next west wall tile repaint their shared raster column into two stone-coloured seams across an otherwise continuous mirror — so runtime north and west slot X values are complements around the shared wall-art datum (`x = 42` west and `x = 86` north around `x = 64`), slot Y equal. An asset-projection rule only: board-grid corridors, reflected seats, piece facing, 1:1 scale, wall support and floor occlusion are untouched, and the generation gate fails on any difference | accepted | 2026-07-12 |
| [0088](0088-chrome-candidates-and-installed-roles-are-live-catalog-owned.md) | Chrome review images are non-active backend candidates, source/review/report bytes are private archives, and live Chrome consumes five canonical DB-owned atom/rail/joint slots; Chrome Lab and Rail Lab hydrate candidate/family metadata from the admin catalog rather than committed manifests | accepted; partially supersedes ADR-0084 Git-backed candidate/family storage and generator enforcement | 2026-07-12 |
| [0089](0089-sfx-runtime-profile-is-db-authoritative.md) | One complete revisioned `sfx_profiles/default` document owns sound-set metadata/gains, terrain assignments, and arrival sample/gain/firing. Public GET hydrates runtime and Studio; admin optimistic PUT saves the owner-operated instrument; localStorage is draft-only; a missing profile is honest decorative silence with no Git/default fallback. Recording bytes remain live-media slots and profile Save cannot accept candidates | accepted; partially supersedes ADR-0047 Git/local-copy configuration clauses | 2026-07-12 |
| [0090](0090-private-draft-cards-preview-and-manage-working-copies.md) | The authenticated `/editor` Continue-editing cards are a bounded private working-copy preview and management surface: load displayed owner-scoped documents after summary discovery, render their working Levels through the shared `LevelThumbnail`, CAS-rename without promotion, Discard saved-backed edits, and confirmed-CAS-delete only never-saved documents. Campaign/gameplay/share/server thumbnails remain canonical-only | accepted | 2026-07-12 |
| [0091](0091-unsaved-drafts-belong-to-the-unassigned-editor-collection.md) | Continue-editing working-copy cards render only inside the Editor's Unassigned levels collection; that rail tab uses its existing trailing slot for an accessible unsaved-work `!`, while its numeric subtitle continues to count canonical unassigned Levels only | accepted | 2026-07-13 |
| [0092](0092-dividers-inherit-their-host-chrome-role.md) | Structural dividers remain one repeatable child bar primitive but inherit the rail, thickness, fit, and reach of their `outer` or `inner` host. Chrome Lab v4 owns independent `dividers.outer` / `dividers.inner` geometry including visible band height, exposes Divider controls and arbitrary-N previews for both box roles, and persists validated numeric defaults through a real atomic dev-save boundary. Both roles continue to share the one canonical divider-joint media slot | accepted; partially supersedes ADR-0082/0083/0084 outer-only divider ownership; layout-axis model extended by [0239](0239-run-army-rows-use-a-divided-portrait-bay-and-explicit-type-hierarchy.md) | 2026-07-12 |
| [0093](0093-chrome-rails-own-alignment-atoms-use-clip-aprons.md) | Chrome rails own box and Contents Box alignment; corner atoms remain absolute paint-only overhang. Any scroll or viewport clip that directly contains chrome must provide a transparent measured clip apron with compensating padding, including divider-joint overhang, so tuning never moves the rail or creates horizontal scrolling | accepted; refines ADR-0081/0082/0083/0084 and extends ADR-0092 | 2026-07-13 |
| [0094](0094-merge-builds-and-deploys-the-merged-image.md) | A protected merge to `main` authorizes deployment: PR CI builds commit-addressed validation images, while Build and Deploy tests and builds the exact merged revision, locks its fingerprint tag and manifest, and pins that digest on `prod`. The completed runtime-asset cutover's comment token, candidate-promotion gate, verifier, and runbook are retired end to end | accepted | 2026-07-13 |
| [0095](0095-level-editor-chrome-rules-are-mechanically-enforced.md) | Level Editor chrome rules are mechanically enforced: an AST-aware gate rejects new native selects, unregistered buttons, direct box-role impersonation, and locally painted control types; the same gate runs in frontend checks and CI while named legacy select debt cannot grow | accepted | 2026-07-14 |
| [0096](0096-level-editor-scenic-terrain-apron-is-decoration-only.md) | The Level Editor persists independently tunable top/right/bottom/left Scenic terrain extents that extend nearest-edge terrain tops for art handoff, while freezing decorative animation; apron coordinates are render-only and never become gameplay cells, placement targets, features, zones, solver state, or Level terrain | accepted; placement restrictions partially superseded by ADR-0098, nearest-available synthesis plus separate-canvas clauses partially superseded by ADR-0126, and gameplay/thumbnail visibility clause superseded by [0284](0284-board-views-render-the-complete-authored-visual-scene.md) | 2026-07-14 |
| [0097](0097-decorative-features-use-separate-non-gameplay-channels.md) | Road and Fence tools may author visual-only scenic roads/rails/posts around the playable grid; Walls independently author north/west decorative faces. Separate persisted channels reuse canonical renderers but never project into gameplay terrain, barriers, movement, collision, or solver state | superseded by ADR-0098 | 2026-07-15 |
| [0098](0098-authored-board-extends-beyond-playable-grid.md) | The Level Editor owns one authored visual rectangle; ordinary terrain, feature, barrier, prop, doodad, and cover tools work across it, while the playable rectangle alone projects gameplay terrain, units, zones, collision, movement, objectives, promotion, and solver state. Supersedes ADR-0097 and ADR-0096's outer-placement prohibition | accepted; off-board prop and doodad authoring permission partially superseded by [0176](0176-placed-art-and-level-artwork-are-separate-editor-destinations.md) | 2026-07-15 |
| [0099](0099-title-bar-and-control-panels-form-one-outer-rail.md) | The persistent title bar, Play controls, and Level Editor controls form one outer-role rail system. The bespoke forged title-bar band is retired; trailing-menu and title/control seams use outer divider joints; the vertical gutter is zero; and the right control box meets the viewport edge with paint-only bottom atoms flush to its rail | superseded by ADR-0100; supersedes ADR-0037 | 2026-07-15 |
| [0100](0100-title-and-controls-are-one-branched-rail-topology.md) | Play and Level Editor use one branched outer-rail topology: title owns top/sides/top corners, one outer divider is both title bottom and controls top, controls own sides/bottom/bottom corners, and authored divider joints cover internal rail branches | accepted; supersedes ADR-0099; exterior-corner clause superseded by ADR-0103 | 2026-07-15 |
| [0101](0101-title-bar-buttons-use-the-inner-box-role.md) | Every persistent title-bar button is a registered inner-box consumer; the inner role owns frame art and active treatment while title-specific CSS owns content geometry. Raw action-slot buttons and persistent controls that bypass the canonical primitive fail repository checks | accepted; routed caller-access mechanism superseded by [0104](0104-title-bar-controls-are-typed-contributions-to-one-lane.md); supersedes ADR-0023/0036 mode-button frame details | 2026-07-15 |
| [0102](0102-runtime-buttons-use-registered-inner-chrome.md) | Retire mode-button frames from runtime application chrome: every former app-header-button consumer declares a registered inner unit, the legacy class is layout-only, the app root exposes one chrome-family scope, and checks reject unowned consumers or direct retired-frame references anywhere in runtime source | accepted; generalizes ADR-0101 | 2026-07-15 |
| [0103](0103-viewport-edge-title-rails-have-no-corner-atoms.md) | The shared title bar always uses the cornerless viewport-edge treatment: exterior rails flow beyond the visible canvas without turning along screen edges or carrying corner atoms; internal rail intersections retain divider-junction atoms. The route-local chromeCorners review switch is retired | accepted; supersedes ADR-0100 exterior-corner clause | 2026-07-15 |
| [0104](0104-title-bar-controls-are-typed-contributions-to-one-lane.md) | Routed title-bar controls are closed typed contributions rendered by one App-owned control lane: contributed controls, persistent divider, music, settings, and account share alignment, gaps, and edge clearance; arbitrary action-slot JSX and caller-owned placement are retired and mechanically rejected | accepted; supersedes ADR-0042 actionsSlot and ADR-0101 routed caller-access mechanisms; fulfills ADR-0100 shared-topology ownership | 2026-07-18 |
| [0105](0105-subterrain-is-an-opt-in-drawable-surface.md) | Subterrain is an independent opt-in vertical drawable surface with its own editor layer, persistence, and render path; tiles never own, imply, derive, or default a vertical side | accepted; supersedes ADR-0087 material fallback and ADR-0039 tile-owned side layer | 2026-07-18 |
| [0106](0106-installed-content-is-database-owned.md) | Git owns behavior and schemas; Postgres owns every installed content record and its configuration; editors, catalogs, renderers, validation, browser bakes, and server thumbnails query one logical drawable projection rather than compiling inventories or constructing media slots | accepted; partially supersedes code-owned persistence/catalog clauses in ADR-0016, ADR-0019, ADR-0039, ADR-0054, ADR-0061, ADR-0083, and ADR-0085 | 2026-07-19 |
| [0107](0107-predrawn-registration-has-no-unsaved-dialog-state.md) | Pre-drawn registration has no unsaved dialog transaction: every complete click, nudge, or restore writes through immediately to the temporary review URL and enables the grid; the final action only closes the picker | accepted; immediate-write/no-save-action clause superseded by [0108](0108-predrawn-registration-is-local-first-and-explicitly-saved.md) | 2026-07-13 |
| [0108](0108-predrawn-registration-is-local-first-and-explicitly-saved.md) | Pre-drawn candidate registration is committed by an explicit Save Registration action that synchronously writes a source-scoped local record, reads back and verifies the exact value before reporting success, then mirrors the URL and enables the grid; same-browser local state outranks the URL | accepted; partially supersedes ADR-0107's immediate-write/no-save-action clause | 2026-07-13 |
| [0109](0109-predrawn-generation-packets-preserve-authored-level-semantics.md) | Whole-level pre-drawn generation uses an authored-level packet: an exact unit-free geometry guide owns spatial layout, a canonical tile/edge/footprint/perimeter dump owns semantics, and separately labeled art references own appearance only; the boundary location is fixed while its visual treatment remains generative | accepted | 2026-07-13 |
| [0110](0110-owner-fitted-grid-defines-predrawn-review-rectification.md) | Pre-drawn review exposes the complete logical grid over the untouched candidate: four corners place the plane, monotonic row/column handles fit its internal spacing, a distortion readout makes the correction inspectable, and one inverse warp applies that saved fit to the continuous painting | accepted; fixed authored-dimension target superseded by [0111](0111-predrawn-refit-target-dimensions-are-owner-configurable.md), production emits the immutable raster child required by [0158](0158-immutable-predrawn-background-versions-own-derived-raster-and-occlusion.md), and row/column-only correction scope is partially superseded by [0171](0171-local-predrawn-grid-correction-uses-a-shared-vertex-mesh.md) | 2026-07-13 |
| [0111](0111-predrawn-refit-target-dimensions-are-owner-configurable.md) | Pre-drawn calibration lets the owner set the row and column count of the refit target itself; saved guides and homography use that exact target while the canonical playable level remains unchanged and exposes any generated extra rows or columns | accepted; post-picker review-grid clause superseded by [0112](0112-predrawn-review-overlay-uses-the-saved-refit-grid.md), while production emits the immutable raster child required by [0158](0158-immutable-predrawn-background-versions-own-derived-raster-and-occlusion.md) | 2026-07-13 |
| [0112](0112-predrawn-review-overlay-uses-the-saved-refit-grid.md) | While a temporary pre-drawn candidate registration is active, the visible post-picker review grid uses the saved refit dimensions; gameplay cells and hit targets remain authored-level geometry | accepted; partially supersedes ADR-0111's authored-size post-picker grid | 2026-07-13 |
| [0113](0113-predrawn-calibration-can-snap-to-the-canonical-grid-shape.md) | Pre-drawn calibration can snap the currently selected refit dimensions to the exact canonical board angles, cell aspect, and equal spacing while preserving the current center/scale and leaving authored gameplay geometry unchanged | accepted | 2026-07-13 |
| [0114](0114-predrawn-calibration-keeps-an-independent-pinned-boundary.md) | Pre-drawn calibration can persist an owner-fitted four-line painted-boundary reference independently of the working ideal grid; snapping and editing the grid never move the reference, and the reference never changes rendering or gameplay geometry | accepted | 2026-07-13 |
| [0115](0115-predrawn-registration-handoff-is-a-compact-copy-packet.md) | After a pre-drawn registration is explicitly saved and read-back-verified, the calibration instrument copies a compact source-plus-registration JSON packet for browser-independent owner-to-agent handoff; the mirrored editor URL is no longer the handoff format | accepted; required installation handoff partially superseded by [0158](0158-immutable-predrawn-background-versions-own-derived-raster-and-occlusion.md), so export is optional diagnostic/provenance only | 2026-07-14 |
| [0116](0116-registered-predrawn-candidates-activate-the-locked-editor.md) | A valid saved registration plus an allowed same-origin development candidate activates the real Level Editor's pre-drawn mode before live-media acceptance: the complete plate renders under the live grid, every baked-art mutation is disabled and rejected, additive overlays remain editable, and temporary source metadata is never persisted | accepted; temporary-source activation and additive-environment exceptions partially superseded by [0158](0158-immutable-predrawn-background-versions-own-derived-raster-and-occlusion.md) | 2026-07-14 |
| [0117](0117-predrawn-scenes-own-a-viewport-cover-zoom-floor.md) | A pre-drawn scene's transformed full-frame boundary and the live ViewPane dimensions derive its zoom-out floor; every wheel, editor, HUD, shortcut, and reset path shares that floor, and the ordinary cap rises when necessary so the complete painting continues to cover the viewport | superseded by [0121](0121-predrawn-pan-stops-at-art-boundary.md) | 2026-07-14 |
| [0118](0118-predrawn-scenes-generate-a-camera-overscan-frame.md) | Pre-drawn scenes generate an exact 3840x2160 camera-overscan frame with the registered grid and immediate boundary inside the centered 60%; more pixels without more surrounding composition does not create pan travel | superseded by [0123](0123-accepted-predrawn-scenes-keep-their-pixels-and-saved-alignment.md) | 2026-07-14 |
| [0119](0119-predrawn-generation-is-tested-without-prior-candidates.md) | The default pre-drawn generation test runs without prior candidates, beauty renders, accepted plates, or unrelated style images: one canonical grass-free top-only render supplies visual authority, the serialized level packet supplies semantics, and text supplies finish and atmosphere | superseded by [0120](0120-canonical-top-only-image-owns-predrawn-appearance.md); partially supersedes ADR-0109 appearance-reference allowance | 2026-07-14 |
| [0120](0120-canonical-top-only-image-owns-predrawn-appearance.md) | The canonical grass-free top-only render owns geometry and appearance; text owns exact semantics, transformation requirements, and prohibitions but does not name or prescribe biome, palette, lighting, materials, atmosphere, or style | accepted; source-image restriction partially superseded by [0165](0165-ai-artwork-separates-sources-attempts-and-background-mode.md); top-surfaces-only exclusion partially superseded by [0141](0141-predrawn-generation-references-preserve-explicit-subterrain.md); supersedes [0119](0119-predrawn-generation-is-tested-without-prior-candidates.md) | 2026-07-14 |
| [0121](0121-predrawn-pan-stops-at-art-boundary.md) | Pre-drawn zoom-out uses one stable transformed-frame floor independent of current pan; pan moves until the viewport reaches the actual art edge and then stops, while zoom and resize clamp the camera back inside without raising minimum zoom | accepted; centered-floor clause partially superseded by [0190](0190-accepted-art-zoom-floor-uses-the-full-feasible-pan-region.md); supersedes [0117](0117-predrawn-scenes-own-a-viewport-cover-zoom-floor.md), while [0158](0158-immutable-predrawn-background-versions-own-derived-raster-and-occlusion.md) replaces the runtime-transformed source polygon with the immutable raster version's persisted frame/world bounds | 2026-07-14 |
