| [0534](0534-a-generator-instance-owns-patches-of-ground.md) | A saved Town or Forest owns a LIST of rectangles and its ground is their UNION, extended by shift-dragging another patch onto the selected instance, so a town can bend around a corner or run on past the edge of one screenful of board; `areas` carries the list, `bounds` is derived as its bounding box, a single patch is not written out so pre-existing documents and board codes are byte-identical and need no migration, and a patch another already covers is dropped. Membership (which plots exist, which cells are scattered, where the falloff edge is) reads the union while layout templates are still fitted to rectangles — `mergeGeneratorAreas` collapses end-to-end patches back into one long rectangle so an extended town runs ONE street its whole length, and keeps an L's two arms so each gets a street of its own. Every boundary test was restated so a join is not an edge: footprints test the union's coverage, the town's half-cell inset is applied in cell edges, the Forest's falloff measures along the grid axes through whatever ground is there, and the coarse plot filter stays on the bounding box — two patches that tile a rectangle produce exactly the town and exactly the trees the single rectangle produces | accepted | 2026-08-08 |
| [0535](0535-a-formation-is-drawn-as-one-block-on-the-ground.md) | A Run Deployment formation is drawn as ONE liveried plot — the card face's own footprint outline, stroked only on the edges facing off the shape, on the battlefield's own 96×54 tile geometry — from the moment it is carried and unchanged when it is seated, because ADR-0533 makes both the same plan at the same strength; `formationBlockSquares` delegates to `runCardFormationBoardCells` so board and card cannot disagree, each formation wears a livery keyed to its place in the dealt hand (`RUN_FORMATION_LIVERY_COUNT` 6, against a hand of 3 or 4), and the carried block keeps the livery it will hold with only its ground lifted. Derived from `unitSeats` and `deployment.placements`, so no RunSaveVersion moves; the boundary is drawn at twice weight and halved by the square's own diamond clip, over a dark carrier, because the board already strokes every tile edge dark. Tether, hover-only and a planted standard were rejected | accepted; refines ADR-0533/0492/0526 | 2026-08-08 |
| [0536](0536-a-scroll-rail-is-drawn-only-when-there-is-something-to-scroll.md) | A `<KitScroll>` pane with nothing to scroll draws no rail and reserves no gutter, so its rows run the full width instead of standing clear of a bare groove: the wrapper marks itself `data-kit-scroll-rail="collapsed"`, which stops the paint and zeroes `--kit-scroll-gutter`, and every consumer reserves its space from that token (sized by `--kit-scroll-gutter-size`) rather than a literal; the rail element stays mounted and merely `visibility: hidden` because ADR-0448 measures the thumb's track off its rendered height, a gutter that is a framed grid column with drawn dividers and junctions is exempt so no drawn line moves, and content whose height grows with its width latches to the drawn rail after two flips inside one unchanged-`clientHeight` settling episode | accepted; partially supersedes ADR-0030's always-visible rail and reserved gutter | 2026-08-08 |
| [0537](0537-an-obstacle-stands-on-the-artwork-it-is-not-painted-into-it.md) | A rock may be placed on a pre-drawn board — it STANDS ON the artwork rather than being painted into it, and it is the only prop kind that may, because ADR-0518 established a rock as the obstacle that shapes a position while trees and houses are the scenery the plate already painted; Placed Art stays reachable on a plate and narrows to Props → Rocks, and the rock draws live, blocks, and takes ADR-0518's drop. One `liveProps` marker set over the existing props map rather than a second channel, so occupancy, Move, erase, resize and the `layers.props` projection are untouched — and because a generation reference includes props, an unmarked board keeps rendering exactly as it does today instead of standing a live rock on each painted one. A live obstacle leaves the environment-geometry digest, the baked-art guard, the occlusion seed and the generation reference, and needs no digest schema version because no board could carry a marker before the field existed, so every persisted v1/v2 hash is byte-identical | accepted; partially supersedes ADR-0116's prop clause, refines ADR-0162/0516/0518 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0538](0538-a-level-declares-its-factions.md) | A level DECLARES its factions instead of having them inferred from whatever colours are painted: `FACTION_ROLES` is the pair `player`/`enemy` — the two sides the engine actually fields, since `sideForFaction` maps every non-player palette to one enemy army — and `resolveDeclaredFactions()` is the single answer every faction-facing surface reads (`sideDefaultFaction`, `victoryFactions`, the preview, the brush). Board → **Declared Factions** always shows both roles with the colour each wears, its default facing and its unit count; changing a declared colour repaints that faction's pieces so declaration and board cannot disagree, and picking the other role's colour SWAPS the sides rather than merging them. The Units page picks a faction, never a palette — a `HouseSelect<FactionRole>` replaces the six-colour `PaletteSelect`, so an undeclared colour is unpaintable, and the brush snaps back to the declared player when the declaration moves under it. The enemy half persists as the new optional board-code key `ef` beside `pf`, so a board with no enemy pieces still knows its colour while a board declaring nothing encodes byte-identically. Resolution is pure and never rewrites the document (ADR-0304's read-only load), authoring either half writes both, and legacy three-plus-colour pieces are surfaced with a fold-into-a-declared-faction repair rather than silently rewritten. `FactionControl` and the per-colour Player/CPU dropdown are deleted; the campaign gate now means "the declared player faction fields no units" | accepted; refines ADR-0064's per-faction victory model | 2026-08-08 |
| [0539](0539-par-is-turns-and-the-speed-bonus-is-the-clock.md) | Par and speed are two different things and only one of them is a rule: **par is the level's authored turn budget, in moves** (`Level.parTurns`) — a BENCHMARK and never a rule, so crossing it wins nothing and loses nothing and the victory screen only reports the standing ("5 under par of 14") — while **speed is the clock**, because par sizes a bonus clock of `parTurns × SPEED_BONUS_SECONDS_PER_PAR_TURN` whose remainder at the win pays gold, scaled linearly to a cap. One authored number produces both, so an author tunes moves and never reasons about seconds. That bonus clock deliberately does not reuse `ClockState`: ADR-0053's `timeControl` is LETHAL (`expireClock` loses the Battle at zero), and hanging a reward on a clock that can also end the fight punishes exactly the calculation the genre is about — running this one out costs the bonus and nothing else, which is also why the battlefield shows no countdown, the title-bar chip keeps counting elapsed time upward, and the clock is reported at the end beside every other measure of the fight. `SPEED_BONUS_MAX_TENTHS` is 10 — one gold at a perfect clock, against a 5–10 gold Battle reward, a starting bank of 8 and a mean card price near 3.4 — so it reads as a nudge and a player who plays badly and fast cannot out-earn one who plays well. An unauthored par is ESTIMATED rather than absent (`derivedParTurns`: two turns per enemy plus a small allowance, and a `survive` board's own turn target), labelled as an estimate and always beaten by an authored one; only an authored par is written to the document, so an untuned level re-estimates as its board changes instead of freezing whatever the panel happened to show. The bonus is derived and never stored — `speedBonusTenths(level, elapsedMs)` is pure, `closeBattle` pays with it and the aftermath re-derives the identical number from the report's frozen `elapsedMs`, so `RunAftermathState` gains no field and stays off the backend aftermath allowlist and out of a RunSaveVersion bump — and the elapsed time is read from the wall clock ONCE, in `closeBattle`, because re-reading it when the gold is banked at Continue would pay less than the screen promised. `Level.parTurns` is additive and back-compat (`validateLevel` ignores unknown fields, `LEVEL_FORMAT_VERSION` unchanged, no schema migration); it is authored in the Level Editor's Rules panel beside the battle clock with a Reset back to the estimate (ADR-0057), the final Battle of a War pays no speed bonus because it pays no spendable reward and has no aftermath screen (ADR-0220), and a campaign or skirmish win still displays nothing because those surfaces have no ledger to put it in | accepted | 2026-08-08 |
| [0540](0540-the-board-pays-manubiae-for-named-tactics.md) | The things a Run pays a player for DOING on the board are named **Manubiae** and get one catalog, one `payRunManubium`, one notice shape and one Enchiridion section, answering ADR-0527's "a third would want a shared name before it wants its own machinery"; the two bespoke pay functions are deleted into it. Six entries, cheapest first: advantageous capture (0.2 gold per point of margin — scaled because it is the one deed landed constantly, and exact in tenths), royal fork (1), discovered check (2), double check (3), en passant (5), smothered mate (5). A unit is worth what it STARTED as on both sides of a capture — `Piece.promotedFrom` is stamped by `applyMove`, matching the roster, which has no promotion concept and hands a queened pawn back as a Pawn; King and obstacles have no purchase price and yield no margin. A discovery needs no before-and-after because the enemy King cannot already be in check on the player's turn, so any checker that did not just move is a line this move opened; double check pays INSTEAD of discovered check as one ladder, while every other combination stacks. Smothered mate is kept literal — a lone Knight, no legal move, every in-bounds neighbour the King's OWN man — because it is the one named mating pattern whose definition is local enough to survive non-8x8 boards with terrain. Motifs needing the opponent's reply or the player's intent (deflection, clearance, zwischenzug, zugzwang, sacrifice) are excluded as unpayable. `kingCheckers`/`sideHasLegalMove`/`smotheredMateBy` join `royalForkVictim` in core/rules, and the canonical adjudicator now calls `sideHasLegalMove` instead of keeping its own copy; board law, RunSaveVersion and save shape are untouched | accepted; refines ADR-0193/0517/0525/0527 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0541](0541-a-promotion-premove-is-asked-what-it-becomes-as-it-is-queued.md) | A premove queued onto an authored promotion cell asks what the Pawn becomes AS IT IS QUEUED, beside that step's ghost, and carries the answer on the `PremoveStep` — because a premove exists to spend the opponent's thinking time, and ADR-0503's fire-time question arrived at the one moment the premove was built to protect, then dead-ended the chain at a type nobody had chosen. `PendingPromotion.mode` gains `premove-queue`: it opens straight at `choosing` with no landing phase or landing sound, neither board nor HUD applies `promotionArrivalPieces` (the premove projection already draws the Pawn dimmed at its origin and ghosted on the cell), the picker reads **Premove queued** / **Choose what this Pawn will become**, and answering mutates only the queue. A resolved type restores the rest of the chain — the ghost is the chosen piece and further steps plan from it under ADR-0358 — while Escape drops an unanswered question with its chain and the landing beat is held open rather than firing a step that does not know what it becomes. Fire-time exact legality, the single atomic commit and netplay's one complete ordered intent are untouched; ADR-0503's arrive-then-ask survives unchanged for a played move and as the fallback for a choice-less programmatic step | accepted; partially supersedes ADR-0503's destination-only premove clause, refines ADR-0358/0504 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0542](0542-a-formation-in-hand-is-off-the-board.md) | A Run Deployment formation in the player's HAND is not on the battlefield at all: picking one up takes its units AND its plot off the board, so moving a formation looks exactly like placing it for the first time. ADR-0526 made a seated formation pickable and ADR-0533 draws a seated formation at exactly the strength the carried one is drawn at, so repositioning painted the SAME body twice over, identically, and neither copy said which was being decided; a faint shadow of the old seats was tried and rejected on sight, because half a lift still reads as a formation standing there. In hand is an unplaced card OR a placed one the player picked up — a gesture, not a selection, because placing the last card of a hand leaves it selected and a resting formation must keep standing. The whole carry is gated on it (band, reachable squares, offered turns, cursor formation, placement click), which also stops the just-placed card following the mouse and makes ADR-0526's "the band is painted whenever a formation is in hand" literally true; a resting formation cannot be moved by a click it never offered. Nothing is written to the Run — `deploymentLayoutInHand` and `seatedFormationsBySquare(run, cardInHandId)` are projections over the fields the grouping already reads — so a pick-up costs nothing, walking away restores the old seat, and no RunSaveVersion moves | accepted; refines ADR-0533/0526 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0543](0543-a-mate-is-paid-for-the-army-it-did-not-have-to-take.md) | A won Battle pays **Deditio** — 0.2 gold for each point of enemy force still alive on the committed final board — because every Run Battle is won by checkmate (King Assault's win rule wants the King CAPTURED, which legal-move generation never permits, so `adjudicateCommittedPosition` falls through to mate) while `battleVictoryGoldTenths` is computed from the enemies the level FIELDS rather than the ones taken, so mating on move twelve and grinding sixty moves to a bare King paid identically and grinding was strictly safer. The rate is ADR-0540's advantageous-capture rate on purpose — material won and material never had to be taken are worth the same per point — and against a typical sixteen-point force paying nine gold, mating with the army whole banks 3.2 while a ground-down mate banks nothing, which is exactly what such a mate has always paid, so the floor is today's payout and only the ceiling moves. It is the one reward a player can LOSE by playing longer. The aftermath stores `standingEnemyValue` (the fact) and both the report and the Continue that banks it price it through `deditioGoldTenths`, the ADR-0539 shape and for the same reason: the board is gone by then, so the screen and the banking read one number twice rather than agree by luck. Worth is `manubiaeUnitWorth`, so a promoted enemy pawn surrenders as a Pawn and the King and obstacles count for nothing — which is what makes a stripped King score zero; every living enemy counts, spawned ones included. The Unclaimed Dagger suppresses checkmate until every enemy non-King is dead, so a Run holding it scores zero Deditio permanently and its ten gold finally buys a real cost. RunSaveVersion 36 with both migrations; a Run parked on a report gains the zero it truthfully earned so Continue banks the total already shown. `standing=<n>` joins `turns`/`seconds`/`fallen` on `craft=aftermath`, defaulting to the whole force the level fields because a placed Battle killed nothing. No Enchiridion section — it is a Battle reward like the speed bonus, explained on the ledger where it is paid | accepted; refines ADR-0193/0220/0539/0540 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0544](0544-leaving-a-run-does-not-wait-for-its-account-row.md) | Abandon Run and Finish Run navigate in the same tick the Run is cleared, without awaiting the account's `DELETE /api/active-run` — the store drops the Run from the browser synchronously, so awaiting parked the player on the empty **No active Run** workspace for a round trip plus any queued autosave, and that workspace is what `/run` shows when opened without a Run, not a state any exit should route through. The destination is unchanged (`/play/select/run`, whose disabled Current Run row is ADR-0334's and whose Start New Run is the next action). Navigating early opens a race the `await` had been closing by accident, so `abandon()` now puts its DELETE INSIDE the save chain instead of merely behind it: behind it only stops a queued PUT from resurrecting the abandoned Run, while a replacement Run started seconds later had its first PUT overtaken by the in-flight DELETE and left the account holding nothing against a browser that believed it had a Run — reproduced live on both orderings. `abandon()` still returns its promise and callers that write a REPLACEMENT Run under the same account (Start New Run's own abandon, the War Editor's direct play) still await it; awaiting is for callers that write next, not for callers that leave. A DELETE that does not land reports through the existing persistence message on the picker rather than holding the exit or reinstating the Run | accepted; refines ADR-0290/0334/0428 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0545](0545-the-sides-a-level-fields-have-their-own-page.md) | The pair of sides a level fields gets its own editor layer, **Factions** (`?layer=factions`), instead of riding the bottom of Board's Level Settings card behind the size steppers, the grid nudge, the plate placement and the scenic rectangle. Everything else on Board changes how a level LOOKS; the declaration changes who the human plays as, because `sideForFaction` resolves every unit wearing the declared player colour to `side: 'player'` and everything else to the opposition, and that mapping is baked into the saved Level. That matters most where ADR-0538 is deliberately silent: an unauthored declaration is READ off the pixels (player = first painted palette in catalog order), so a board painted only in Black opens showing "Player 1 · Black" and presents the read exactly as it presents a choice — handing the author the army the level meant them to fight. So the page states it in words ("Player 1 is the army the human commands in play. Every other piece fights it."), and every control rides a LABELLED `.le-ctrlrow` — `Colour`, `Default facing` — the shape the unit brush already used one page over. The compass square finally explains itself: `DirectionPopover` takes an optional `describe` and always appends the current value, so a pointer gets "The way a newly painted Player 1 unit stands. Currently facing south." instead of an `S` with no `title` at all, and the Units page gets the same tooltip from the same change. A blocked `needsPlayerFaction` save lands on Factions rather than Board. Resolution, persistence, repainting, `pf`/`ef` and the board code are untouched — this moves a surface and adds no state; `.le-faction-fields` is deleted and material-by-faction stays on Status as a board readout | accepted; supersedes ADR-0538's "Board → Declared Factions" placement clause only | 2026-08-09 |
| [0546](0546-a-level-already-knows-its-sides.md) | A saved Level's own units are the authority on its sides, and the editor reads them. `Level.layers.units[].side` says which side a piece plays on; `boardCode`'s unit is `{unitId, direction, faction}` and says only which colour it wears — so `levelToEditorBoard` dropped the side, and `resolveDeclaredFactions` fell back to "the first painted palette is the player", which on a board painted in ONE colour names the opposition. The Run test Battle `Boxed in` (five black `side:"enemy"` pieces, nothing declared) opened as **Player 1 · Black · 5**, and because every declaration edit writes both halves (ADR-0538), accepting what the panel showed persisted `pf=black, ef=white` and `sideForFaction` re-derived all five as the player's; a Battle built from that fields the Run roster PLUS five player-sided pieces and no enemy, since `setup.ts` reads `side` verbatim and `levelWithRunDeployment` appends rather than replaces. The mirror failure needed no authoring at all: a single-colour PLAYER army was silently demoted to the enemy by open-and-save, because an unrecovered `playerFaction` makes `sideForFaction` answer `enemy` for everything. `declaredFactionsFromLevelUnits` now derives the pair from `layers.units` and `levelToEditorBoard` fills either half the board did not declare on both the boardCode and legacy paths, deleting the old no-boardCode fallback that read the side but then declared the hard-coded navy `SIDE_TO_FACTION.player` regardless of what the pieces wore. An authored half always wins; a colour worn by BOTH sides names neither (the pre-palette shape, where `paletteOfLevelUnit` gives each side its historical navy/crimson); and recovery is a fixed point because the recovered half rides the next board code, which is also what keeps ADR-0304's read-only load intact — the live candidate and `normalizedLevelEditorSignature` both project through `levelToEditorBoard`, so a recovered half cancels on both sides of the autosave comparison. Separately, `resolveDeclaredFactions` no longer offers a declared enemy half to the player's fallback: it used to compute the player first and only then check for a collision, silently discarding a level that declared only its opposition. No format change, no migration, no `LEVEL_FORMAT_VERSION` or `RunSaveVersion` move — the information was never missing, only unread; a working copy that already carries an inverted declaration is not overwritten, because **Discard changes** restores it from the canonical level that still has the right sides | accepted; refines ADR-0538, narrows its painted-palette fallback to boards with no level behind them | 2026-08-09 |
| [0547](0547-gold-is-whole-and-the-stored-tenth-is-the-gold.md) | One tenth becomes one gold: the integer the Run has always stored IS the gold amount, displayed unchanged, so nothing the player reads has a decimal point any more. `formatGold` printed one or two places whenever `goldTenths / 10` did not come out whole, which was constantly — a Pawn on the winning board pays half its value, Manubiae and Deditio both pay two tenths a point, the speed bonus rounds to a tenth — so the purse read **12.5**, an advantageous capture read **+1.6**, and the smallest payment the economy could make was **0.1**. Raising only the rewards was never available (a card costs its material value, so ten times the income against unchanged prices buys the whole market off one Battle); the only change that kills the decimals and leaves the game identical is to rescale everything by the same ten, and the stored number already WAS that. `GOLD_SCALE` (10) stops being a display divisor and becomes what it always mathematically was — the gold one point of material value is worth — so a 3-point card costs 30, the opening purse is 80, a Pawn pays 5, en passant 50, the royal fork 10, paid Undo 10, a retry 30, Deditio and advantageous capture 2 a point, the speed bonus caps at 10. `cardCostGold(value)` is the single points→gold conversion, at the card-face coin, the Enchiridion's bands and gold filter, and the Level Editor's War-economy readouts. **No stored number changed, so no Run migrated and RunSaveVersion does not move**, and balance is untouched by construction because every price and every award moved by the same factor. Two addresses move with the unit: `craft`'s `gold=` takes whole gold and no longer multiplies (`?gold=250` is the purse the screen shows, a fraction is refused), and the card gallery's filter is written in gold (`?gold=30`, not `?gold=3`; an old single-digit band reads as no filter). Every card cost is now two digits, which the face already handled by digit count. The `Tenths` suffix now lies and keeps its name on purpose — several are fields of the persisted document and the server validator, so renaming needs a schema migration, and a migration cannot be applied locally, which would have made this change unverifiable while it was being made; the block comment at `GOLD_SCALE` is the authority. Material points stay their own unit: `offer.cost`, `card.value`, `RUN_STARTING_GOLD` and the whole `expectedValue` walk are points, because the validator requires `offer.cost === definition.value` and the War economy compares gold against card values | accepted; refines ADR-0220/0517/0527/0539/0540/0543 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0548](0548-a-failed-scene-offers-the-act-that-can-fix-it.md) | A failed scene names the act that can fix it and takes that act itself when it can, because **This scene could not be loaded / Retry** was a dead end in three independent ways after an ordinary dev-server restart. **Retry had never worked**: `retry` returns the director to `loading` and bumps the generation, but the mounted layer key is the scene's identity, so the screen holding the failure — the Level Editor's resolved document error is the canonical one — was re-driven rather than rebuilt and its already-run resolve effect re-reported the same error (verified live: generation 1 → 2 → 3, never reloading). `SceneState.retryEpoch` advances only on `retry` and the layer key carries it, so a retry REBUILDS the failed screen; generation cannot serve because it advances on every navigation and would remount every just-committed screen and its store. The remedy travels ON the error (`sceneFailureError(message, 'sign-in' | 'retry')`) instead of being guessed from its wording, and a screen that declared `sign-in` offers Sign in ALONE — ADR-0519's rule that re-reading a document this account may not have cannot start succeeding however often it is pressed; the Level Editor's 403/404 branch, whose own copy said "Sign in with the account that owns this working copy" while rendering no button at all, now offers it. An UNTAGGED failure under an authoritatively signed-out session offers Sign in BESIDE Retry, never instead of it, because being signed out does not prove the sign-out broke the screen and playing never requires an account (ADR-0060); it leaves through the app's one entry carrying the failing address as `returnTo`. While the failure is on screen the session owner is re-read on a 3s beat plus focus/visibility/`online`, and the scene retries ITSELF when the answer is materially better than the one it failed under — a backend that had stopped answering doing so again, or an identity that moved — while same-backend/same-account/same-failure retries nothing, so a scene broken for its own reasons cannot spin on the beat (`createSceneFailureRecovery`, pure and tested). `refreshAuthSession` now resolves with its own probe's result including a false `reachable` (the published snapshot policy is unchanged — a blip is still not a sign-out — but the caller waiting for a restart needs the one fact of whether the backend answered), and `authSessionIdentityKey` is the owner's comparable name for an identity so no screen derives one (ADR-0306). The actions are registered `inner-text-button` units on the app's one text-button geometry, as `.confirm-actions` does; the bespoke `border-image` rule and its UI-surface debt-baseline entry are deleted and `RouteLoadBoundary` moves with them, while `.app-startup-status` keeps its native button because it renders when the live asset catalog failed and there is no installed chrome to wear | accepted; refines ADR-0102/0205/0306/0519 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0549](0549-exploratio-can-imagine-the-army-onto-the-ground.md) | The Sectio's reconnaissance screen is named **Exploratio** and can deal the held collection onto the deployment band, seated at random, so the band's size can be judged against the army that will stand on it. ADR-0386 wrote the Run army out of the preview on purpose and that reasoning is preserved exactly — the actual hand is settled by `prepareDeployment` from the Run's own seed after the Sectio is left, and the enemy's randomized setup squares stay unresolved — but a ten-square band and a five-square band read identically as a number, which left the ledger raising the question and dropping it. **Shuffle** sits under Zone where the deal count and band size already are, re-seats a different arrangement on every press, reports `3 cards · 7 units`, and **Clear** returns the authored map; the imagining shuffles on the SCREEN's own number rather than the deployment seed, nothing is written to the Run, and the note says in the same breath that this is not the hand you will be given. Every rule it applies is Deployment's — King's card first, whole-card admission while the band has room, and the real placement geometry — so it cannot show an arrangement the Battle would refuse; that meant lifting the phase guard off the geometry rather than writing a second planner (ADR-0059), leaving `formationPlacementOptions` and `distinctFormationRotations` as the rules and `arrangedCardPlacementOptions`/`distinctCardRotations` as those plus the arranging phase's reading of what is admitted and occupied, with `runDeploymentLevelUnits` split out of `levelWithRunDeployment` because a `boardCode` board reads its units from that code and needs the figures rather than a level carrying them. Every distinguishable rotation's seatings go into ONE pool, so the turn is part of the shuffle. The army ARRIVES rather than appearing: it plays ADR-0045's drop on the battlefield's own curve (`arrivalOffset`) and in its own staggered order (`computeArrivalDelays`, royals last), through the read-only renderer's one seam for a motion — `frameTransform`, which the Level Editor already plays a placed obstacle's fall through — so `BoardDrawOp` gains a `unit` identity beside the `structure` one it already carried, for exactly the reason that one exists, and `StudioReadOnlyBoard` forwards the seam it was already built on; the surface stays STILL by default because supplying the transform starts the scene canvas repainting and dropping it when the last unit lands stops it again, so no read-only board gains a permanent clock, and obstacles take no delay because the battlefield seats its obstacles before the armies too. It is deliberately not in the address — a toy for asking a question, not a state worth linking — and is dropped when the map changes. The workspace id and `?view=battle-preview` are unchanged: they are machinery and every craft link that names them still resolves. Separately, every Sectio control gains an installed kit mark from one inventory (`RUN_SECTIO_CONTROL_ICON_ROLE`) — the adlected file for the Sectio, the Run's own Battle mark for Exploratio (the same one its pane header carries), the strike-through for Expunctio, the reset arrow, a drawn sword for continuing into the fighting, the door for abandoning — including Abandon Run on the Deployment rail, which is the same control from a second screen. Making the column taller exposed a latent defect in it: its `auto` grid rows could only grow as far as grid's maximize-tracks step had free space for, so every section rendered past the row it was given and Time printed through the note underneath it while the column reported nothing to scroll; `grid-auto-rows: max-content` restores a real overflow for the installed scrollbar. No RunSaveVersion move — nothing here is persisted | accepted; partially supersedes ADR-0386's no-army-projection clause and its View Battle name, refines ADR-0059/0393/0533 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0550](0550-a-right-click-that-never-panned-takes-the-premove-chain-back.md) | A secondary press on the battle board that releases without panning takes the whole premove chain back — the cancel every chess client already has, previously reachable only by Escape — while a press that panned stays pure navigation. The line `ViewPane`'s `onSecondaryClick` seam draws is restated as committed vs uncommitted rather than destructive vs non-destructive: the gesture may take back a prediction the player has not played (no move made, nothing captured, no clock moved, rebuilt by the same clicks), and may never commit, capture, spend, or erase, so ADR-0128's refusal to put an authored-content erase behind a movement threshold stands unweakened; Deployment's formation turn still owns the button while a formation is in hand, the take-back runs the same `clearPremoves` Escape runs, it is bound to the release rather than `contextmenu` (which fires at the end of a pan too), and with nothing queued it changes no state at all | accepted; refines ADR-0128/0526 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0551](0551-a-sectio-admits-one-card.md) | A Sectio admits exactly **one** card: `performAdlectio` refuses a second admission however much gold remains, so the refusal is the rule and not the price, and `sectioAdlectioSpent` is the one answer every surface asks. ADR-0323 had removed an inherited cap for two sound reasons — it was not communicated, and the room should read as an ordinary shop — but an acquisition rate governed only by gold compounds: a Run that clears a row wins more easily, wins pay more, and the next row is cleared outright, so the force that reaches Battle 5 is nothing like the force Battle 5 was authored against, and card cost, victory gold and Battle difficulty are all tuning a curve whose shape is set by how many cards a visit absorbs. The Sectio's other two transactions were already once-per-visit (Expunctio strikes one card, the After-Hours Key sells one lipsanon); the card row was the only shelf. The count is now stated BEFORE the choice, by the row itself rather than by anything that pops up: *They require compensation. Only one may be admitted.* stands over the row for the whole visit, and when the admission is spent a padlock — the installed kit `ui/kit/icons/lock.png`, not a mark drawn for this row — is laid on every surviving offer as a third layer registered in `RunCardPile`'s own seat, through the `ui-kit-icons-lock-png` app-ui role the Level Editor's own lock uses, sized as a share of the card, inert, and supplied for the whole visit but concealed until it locks (a lock mounted at the moment of locking is fetched then too), so the two together say "you get one, and this is the one you took" with no notice, status box or state-changing copy. Locking is ONE gesture, every part of it at the speed a Run card already moves — the hover, the only place the screen had said how fast one of these objects travels: the padlock comes down onto the face and fades up on the hover RAISE (same duration, same curve, and the same distance, since the descent is the tuned `--run-card-hover-raise` read backwards, bound to one pair of variables so the Studio cannot tune the two apart), while the card's drift settles on the hover SETTLE through the seat's own registered var with the animation paused rather than deleted (deleting it snaps a mid-drift card onto its seat) and the gold emanation leaves on that same timing — `--run-card-glow` is registered for that, which changes nothing since every use already read it as `var(--run-card-glow, 1)`. Appearing at full strength on its final centre had given a heavy object no arrival at all. The pointer takes the ordinary arrow rather than `not-allowed`, whose barred circle rebukes a reach the card has already answered (that covers the unaffordable offer too). That answers ADR-0323's communication failure directly. Unbought offers stay face up and unfaded (cards are printed art, never faded controls) and merely stop being takeable; Reset Sectio returns the admission to the visit, so one card is a decision and not a misclick. Quartermaster's Ledger gains weight: a fourth revealed card is a fourth candidate for the one admission. No save format moves — `adlectedCardOfferIds` simply holds at most one — so an ADR-0323 document sitting in a Sectio with two admissions stays legal and playable and only cannot admit another; `craft`'s `cards=` still stages any number of held cards because it withdraws each staged offer before staging the next | accepted; supersedes [0323](0323-run-shops-allow-every-affordable-card-purchase.md); refines ADR-0392/0393/0494 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0552](0552-athetize-is-a-card-struck-through.md) | Athetize gets a mark, and the mark is a card struck through: a navy card face in a gold filigree border crossed corner to corner by one bold red X, so the destructive act a player performs on their own held formation stops carrying its whole meaning in a deliberately unfamiliar word (ADR-0443). The subject is OUR card rather than a stock playing card or a bare delete glyph, per ADR-0035's world-object vocabulary, and the red belongs to the act — this is the button that already wears the `danger` tone. The card it draws is the card the game DEALS: the Chartulary's mark in the Strategikon is the player's live card back, one of seven resolved through `runCardBackMediaUrl` so a card in flight and the register it lands in cannot disagree about what a card looks like, which means a mark striking a differently drawn card contradicts the register it removes from; the first candidates invented a pale navy card with a thin gold frame and read as generic beside the game's ornate ones, so the accepted family is generated from the standard back as its style reference. It stays ONE installed raster rather than compositing an X over the live back — this mark names an action, not a card instance, and the single-raster contract is what keeps it trimmed, tokenized and installable through the ordinary review — so the mark matches the standard back and a player on one of the other six sees a card of the same deck rather than their exact chosen one. The mark belongs to the CONTROL, not the screen: Expunctio already titles the workspace, so what the button needs named is its effect, which makes the seat a new shared `RunActionIcon` primitive beside `RunProgressIcon` and puts the slot in the action family at `ui/kit/icons/game/athetize.png` rather than with the Run-position marks at `ui/kit/icons/run/`. The seat is reserved rather than fail-closed (ADR-0318) so it holds its geometry before any art decision exists and installing one later cannot shift the label beside it, and it rides every state of that button — offered, spent, refused — because it is one control with one meaning and the disabled styling already says which state it is in. The seat's SIZE is derived from the control it rides rather than asserted: it fills that control's content box and is held off the drawn frame by one `--ds-space-1` step on the block axis, the inline axis already clearing the frame by the host's own `--ds-inset` (ADR-0055) — stating the block step is what makes the mark sit symmetrically inside the frame instead of flush against it, because text gets away with a zero block inset where a line box carries its own leading and a raster is ink to its edge; on the Expunctio button (46px tall, 7px frame, `0 var(--ds-inset)`) that resolves to a 24px seat with 4px clear top and bottom, where a first pass asserting 32px was exactly the content box and touched the frame. The mark ships trimmed to its own ink and padded to the square that bounds it, the finish ADR-0366 gave the measures, so one seat size draws whatever is installed and no per-icon compensating transform exists to drift; `run-action-icon` registers as a trimmed game-condition icon in the live-media policy, accepting a square raster from 16x16 through 64x64 whose runtime frame equals its own side. The PIXELS are the owner's call, not the agent's: candidates are judged in Studio > Action Marks, a catalog category reached by CLICKING its tab, with every candidate on one page and each mounted in the real Expunctio button in both states it has, nothing installed until the owner installs one, and Install sits in the controls rail as the whole decision in one act — record approval of those exact bytes, accept the version into its slot, bind the slot to its `app-ui` role. There is deliberately NO `?athetizeCandidate=<sha256>` seam and the review is not a URL-only route: both were built first and both were wrong under ADR-0058, because a dev surface is a Studio category reachable by clicking rather than an address the owner is handed, and a candidate parameter additionally makes a PLAYER route carry review state it has no business carrying while turning a comparison into one page load per candidate — the equivalent parameters elsewhere are grandfathered debt, not precedent to copy. Candidates came from PixelLab `create_image_pro` off a style reference composited from the three accepted Run marks, trimmed by `trimToInkSquare` (crop and pad only, so the bytes stay honestly native 1x); transparent-background generation hollowed out the card's flat interior on the first portrait attempt, so the accepted portrait candidates were generated on uniform chroma green and keyed. The owner selected Option 08 — densest ornament, coolest gold, nearest the real card back at seat size — and it is installed (content e294c24e…adb9d88, native 64x64, accepted into the slot and bound to `ui-kit-icons-game-athetize-png`), so every Athetize button paints it while the other seven stay uploaded and unaccepted for comparison; before that selection the seat rendered empty, which was the reserved state working rather than a missing asset, and that contract still holds for the next action variant added here. No Run document, database, or save-format migration follows — presentation plus one additive live-media slot, recoverable by retiring it | accepted; refines ADR-0035/0318/0366/0443 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0553](0553-expunctio-says-which-formations-this-sectio-admitted.md) | Expunctio prints **Adlected this Sectio** beside a glyph of the admission on every formation the visit admitted, and says nothing else in words. Reset Sectio restores the complete visit, so a just-bought card can be handed back for nothing while a carried-in one can only leave at the printed fee — and nothing on the tile said which was which, since a just-bought formation and a long-held one of the same composition print the same number, leaving the comparison the screen exists to support to whatever the player remembered buying. `sectioAdmittedCardIds(run)` is the single answer, diffing the held cards against `RunSectioState.entrySnapshot` rather than reading `acquiredAfterBattleIndex`, which numbers the Sectio a card came from and is renormalized by craft and migration (a Run crafted at Battle 4 holds cards stamped 0); the entry snapshot is what Reset restores from, so the mark and the reset cannot disagree, and an empty snapshot yields nothing because no Run holds zero cards. The card struck this visit is included — admitted and athetized by one visit is still that visit's doing, and it is the whole truth about a round trip Reset would refund entirely. Everything else the companion said is deleted with it: the **attached-unit count** is left over from the era when a seat could be emptied one unit at a time (ADR-0511 made a held card one immutable formation, so the count reports something undecidable), the repeated **card name** duplicated the face that prints it — the duplication ADR-0446 removed once and which had quietly returned — and the per-tile **"Athetize removes this card and every attached unit as one formation"** restated what the workspace's rules copy states once at the top. What remains beside the face is the mark, the fee and the action, so an ordinary carried-in formation says nothing in words at all. The mark carries the tile's own ink rather than the muted register the supporting labels use, because it reports state instead of labelling a field, and it answers ADR-0443's *Athetized this visit*. The line says nothing about the price: a coin stood beside the words while the art was undecided and said only that gold was involved, which the fee directly beneath the line already says exactly, and a transaction mark is worse than redundant, since the fee paints `lose-gold`’s arrow and a second arrowed mark reads as a second price while `gold-gained` says the opposite of what happened. The admission gets the new `ui/run/sectio/adlectio-mark.png` slot, since the kit had no glyph for it — `game/adlected` belongs to the retired unit-ability vocabulary and depicts pikes — and the slot is named for WHAT IT MARKS rather than what it draws, because that is an open question with two live answers (the hand that handed the gold over, or the hand that took the card it bought) and both audition in the one seat, so choosing between them is choosing an image and not a code path. The seat draws nothing until a candidate is installed, instead of reserving an empty box, so the line is then the words alone; the installed glyph is a hand pulling the card toward the player, which is the act the words name and the reason the coin could go. Candidates are auditioned in the Studio’s **Adlectio Mark** category, which mounts every one of them in this exact line — the same `RunAdlectioMarkLine` the tile renders — on one page and installs the chosen one, because a review surface is a Studio category reached by clicking (ADR-0058) and never a review parameter bolted onto a player route; one page is also one comparison, where an address per candidate makes the owner do the navigating the surface exists to do. Installing one needs a typed backend projection, since `ui-kit` candidates are bridge-only by default: `adlectioMarkMediaIssue` states the contract the seat depends on — the bytes are trimmed to their own ink, because a seat drawing with `contain` scales the canvas and transparent margin comes straight off the glyph — with no fixed dimensions, as the candidates are hands, cards and coins. Presentation over existing state: nothing is written, no document field is added, RunSaveVersion does not move | accepted; partially supersedes ADR-0446's "status, attached units, fee, and action remain" clause; refines ADR-0407/0442/0443/0511 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0554](0554-a-dead-position-is-a-draw-on-a-board-of-nothing-but-squares.md) | A dead position is a draw, and the board decides whether the material may say so. ADR-0072 audited full chess parity as six items and shipped all six, but the audit was one short: FIDE Article 5.2.2 ends a game the moment checkmate becomes impossible by any series of legal moves, and nothing in the engine said it — so King against King, or King and one minor against King, walked forever on every level that never authored a **Chess draws** event, which is every Run Battle. Stalemate is not the escape hatch (two lone Kings on an open board always have a move) and the 50-move clock is opt-in and, on those levels, absent. `rules.materialCannotMate` is the FIDE table exactly — K vs K, K and one minor vs K, K and Bishop vs K and Bishop on the same colour complex — with **King and two Knights deliberately left running**, because a helpmate exists and that is the exact line FIDE draws (ADR-0072 parity), and with a requirement of one living King per side, since boards here may field more than one and two Kings mate a lone King. The second gate is the owner's question and the reason this is an ADR: the table is not a statement about material but about material **on an empty rectangle**, so `rules.boardIsAllSquares` requires no obstacle pieces, no edge fences, and no terrain a move can feel — nothing impassable, no water, a layer uniformly at elevation 0 — because a wall beside the defending King lets a lone Bishop or Knight mate and the material then proves nothing. Obstacles are authored at build and never appear, move, or clear mid-game, so the answer holds for a whole match; the board scan sits behind the O(pieces) material test, which returns on the first Pawn, so it only ever runs in a bare endgame. The third gate answers ADR-0072's standing objection to an always-on draw rule by reading the rules in force (ADR-0064) rather than hard-coding a mode: a `turnLimit` keeps running whatever the material, so a **Survive** level never draws on a dead position and still wins by outlasting; eliminating a side's King or its whole force can never complete, because both survivors are a lone King and a King is never left en prise; `reach` is pawn-only and a dead position holds no Pawn; while an `eliminate` aimed at a Bishop or Knight stays reachable, since a King can walk up and take an undefended minor — so those levels keep playing. A rule carrying any unreachable condition is dead itself, and the draw is called when no winner-declaring rule survives. It lives in `core/adjudication` after checkmate/stalemate and before the authored chess draws, so every consumer inherits it from the one adjudicator — live play, the netplay relay, self-play, the AI's terminal scoring, the solver's `terminalOutcome` — and it is deliberately NOT a `DrawRules` flag with nothing to author: the other two draws carry hidden ledger state (a clock, a repetition table), which is why they are opt-in and why the board solver refuses boards authoring them, whereas a dead position is a pure function of the position, adds no ledger, joins no `positionKey`, and leaves that refusal exactly where it was. `dead-position` joins the draw kinds on the wire (`LobbyGameResultReason`, the backend's `LOBBY_DRAW_REASONS`) so netplay reports the specific reason, and both seats compute it from the same committed position. No `GameState` field, no serialized change, no RunSaveVersion move; boards WITH obstacles keep exactly today's behaviour, which is not a gap left open — there the material genuinely does not prove the position dead, and proving it is a solver question rather than a table lookup | accepted; refines ADR-0064/0072 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0555](0555-the-controls-panel-wears-the-leaf-material-and-a-portrait-keeps-its-scene.md) | The one shell **Controls** panel adopts ADR-0433's material hierarchy, and it adopts it from the REGISTRY rather than from call sites: `ChromeUnitSpec.material` marks each unit `leaf` or `structural`, and `chromeFamilyRuntime` paints every leaf unit inside `[data-shell-controls-panel]` with `hybrid-wood-oak`. That is what reaches the controls the panel only BORROWS — the Stepper keys, AdminControls, the Run lifecycle buttons — without reskinning those same components on Settings or the Studio, which is the silent whole-app reskin ADR-0433 declined and the reason `ChromeButton` was not simply given the surface by default. The Battle rail and the Level Editor rail are one component, so they adopt together. `inner-box` and `inner-locked-rectangle` stay structural as a container and a template base, and `inner-list-row` stays structural because ADR-0433 already put operation rows there — a row's actions are the leaves, not the row. The phase index collapses to one token pair (`--chrome-leaf-surface-index`/`--chrome-leaf-surface-pitch`) and ONE derivation of `--chrome-surface-position-y`, deleting the `.run-meta-controls` and `.run-army-profile` copies, with `leafSurfacePhase(index)` the shared way a renderer states the index its data already has (tab strip and command card from their own map index, the stepper's − / + from the component that owns the pair) so no wood restarts at the same origin and no ladder is read off DOM position (ADR-0063). A control that names its own surface is EXCLUDED from the role and leaf rules rather than out-specified by them — an `!important` default that merely wins a specificity race is one selector edit from swapping places, which is exactly what happened mid-change when the Sectio rail briefly lost its oak. Separately the framed unit portrait gets its scene back: ADR-0082's guard against local raw-CSS surface paint was stripping the installed portrait backdrop off every FRAMED bust — the roster thumbnails and the Selected Unit seat — while the unframed Prosopography portrait of the same unit kept it, so the guard is split into frame (every inner box) and field (`.inner-box:not(.has-backdrop)`), and `UnitPortrait` now resolves the scene from its piece so the ONE portrait renderer owns it and a call site cannot forget (`backdrop={null}` is the deliberate opt out). The empty Selected Unit seat wears the leaf material, being a terminal identity plate with no scene to stand in. Presentation only — no document, database, or save-format change | accepted; refines ADR-0063/0082/0102/0433 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0556](0556-undo-walks-a-battle-back-a-decision-at-a-time.md) | Paid Run Battle **Undo** takes back one committed player move per press for as far back as the Battle goes, retiring ADR-0394's one-level limit while leaving everything else that ADR decided intact — same checkpoint contents, same capture instant, ADR-0394's unchanged price a press, still no Redo, still no move financing its own Undo. The board store keeps one checkpoint per committed move as a stack and pops it; the price, not a second wall behind it, is what governs how far a player may walk back, so the history ends where the purse does. A checkpoint older than one just restored is charged for the Undo that reached it (`chargeRunBattleUndoCheckpoint`), because a checkpoint photographs the purse its move was played from and restoring one verbatim would refund every gold already spent getting there — a whole Battle rewound for only what its last step cost; the floor is an empty purse, never a debt. Pricing stays in the Run: the board store owns the history and names no price, reaching the economy through `RunBattleUndoAdapter.chargeEarlier` and continuing to take only types from `run/model`. A move committed with no checkpoint drops the whole history rather than leaving a shallower one, since the moves below it could only be reached by rewinding through a move nothing recorded. Everything that cleared the single checkpoint now empties the stack, unchanged otherwise. The history persists with the match snapshot (`undoStack`, storage version 4, a v3 snapshot resuming one deep) so a reload does not silently shorten the offer, and it stays browser-owned rather than joining `RunDocument` — no RunSaveVersion move. A quota-refused write sheds the oldest half and retries until the board fits, because the snapshot is now the only part of that record that grows without bound and the old failure abandoned the write and left a stale board on disk | accepted; supersedes ADR-0394's one-level limit only | 2026-08-09 |
| [0557](0557-a-result-screen-wears-the-leaf-material-and-the-field-excludes-it.md) | The screens a Battle or a Run ENDS on adopt ADR-0433's material hierarchy, and the role FIELD default finally EXCLUDES an adopted leaf instead of losing a tie to it. The Run's board-visible **Victory** banner put a teal `Rewards ›` on the grass, the aftermath report titled **VICTORY** put a teal **Continue** under a stone ledger, the won War put a teal **Finish Run** on the vista, and the result cards beside them — Run Battle Defeat/Draw, campaign, netplay and its persistent exit — were teal for the same reason: no result screen had ever been a rollout destination. Chasing it exposed the larger fault. ADR-0555 painted leaves inside `[data-shell-controls-panel]` at `(0,4,0) !important` and never excluded them from the inner role field, winning the tie on SOURCE ORDER alone; [#881](https://github.com/romaine-life/chess-tactics/pull/881) then added a third `:not()` to that field to rescue the rail tabs, taking it to `(0,6,0)`, and the Controls panel silently lost its oak one day after ADR-0555 shipped it — tab strip, Board View toggles, command keys, Run lifecycle buttons, on the Battle rail and the Level Editor rail both (measured live: the leaf rule matches and is overruled by `background-image: none !important`). The field rule's own comment has named that trap twice — *exclude, do not out-specify* — and twice the exclusion was completed for the case at hand while the next case was left winning on a tie. So adoption gets ONE name rather than one attribute per destination: `data-chrome-leaf-surface` marks a surface that has taken the hierarchy wholesale, `leafSurfaceHostCss()` replaces `controlsPanelLeafSurfaceCss()` and paints from that attribute alone, and the field default gains `:not([data-chrome-leaf-surface] :is(<leaf selectors>))` built from the same `chromeUnitMaterialSelectors('leaf')` the paint rule builds from — so the field no longer MATCHES an adopted leaf and the two cannot trade places on a specificity edit. A per-destination attribute was rejected precisely because it grows that exclusion list once per destination, which is the maintenance shape that produced the regression. The destination is every result screen at once, because they are one family met in one moment and a half-adopted family reads as a bug the first time a Battle is lost; the two Run scenes adopt from their VIEW rather than a call site (`RunSceneViewport` stamps the attribute for `aftermath` and `victory`, the data it already has, ADR-0063), and repeated action rows phase their wood from the action's authored seat via `leafSurfacePhase(index)`, for which the three borrowed Run battle buttons gain a `style` pass-through beside the `className` they already forwarded — host adoption being the only mechanism that reaches a borrowed component without reskinning it everywhere. `Finish Run` additionally takes `justify-self: start`: the workspace lane stretches its grid children, which was inconspicuous under the flat field and reads as a wall the moment it wears a plank. Presentation only — no document, database, or save-format change | accepted; refines ADR-0433/0555/0063 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0558](0558-a-scene-being-replaced-is-not-rebuilt-on-its-way-out.md) | A scene keeps ONE React mount identity from the moment it is committed until the moment it is replaced. Pressing Rewards on a settled Victory flickered because the outgoing layer of a scene replacement was keyed `sceneLayerKey(current)` while the settled single layer was keyed `${sceneLayerKey(current)}#${retryEpoch}` — so the instant a replacement began, the key of the layer holding the VISIBLE screen changed, and React answered that by destroying the battlefield and mounting a second copy. The board vanished, its own "Preparing battlefield…" card appeared under the Victory heading, the board came back, and only THEN did the crossfade start; every scene replacement on every route did this, Victory being merely where the rebuild is expensive enough to announce itself. The destination epoch is load-bearing (ADR-0548 rebuilds a screen holding its own failure) and belongs to the DESTINATION, so the committed scene cannot borrow it — a retry advances it while the committed scene stands painted behind the failure. The director now records `committedEpoch`, the epoch the committed scene was mounted with, advancing only where a destination is promoted to current; which epoch a layer carries follows from which scene it MOUNTS, not which slot it occupies, which also stops a navigation issued during an in-flight retry rebuilding the outgoing scene mid-exit. `npm run verify:scene-retention` is the live gate, asserting DOM mount identity rather than pixels | accepted; refines ADR-0548/0369 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0559](0559-a-promotion-asks-as-the-move-is-played-not-as-it-lands.md) | A promotion asks what the Pawn becomes in the frame the move is AUTHORED, not the frame it lands. ADR-0503 gated the picker on the 360ms player-move glide so the board would show cause before asking for consequence; played rather than read, that is a stall, because the promotion is the player's own intent — they chose that square because it promotes, and the interface made them watch an animation they had already committed to before it would accept the click they were waiting to make. ADR-0541 had already reached the opposite answer one gesture over (a premove is asked as it is queued, since it exists to spend the opponent's thinking time), so the two gestures were asking the same question on two different clocks; they now share one rule. `stagePromotionArrival` sets its pending promotion straight to `choosing` and no timer stands between the gesture and the picker: the arrival projection was already immediate, so the callout anchors to the destination seat from the first frame and the Pawn glides in underneath it. The intermediate phase is DELETED rather than left unreachable — `PendingPromotion.phase` is `choosing | submitted` and `PROMOTION_CHOICE_REVEAL_MS` is gone, leaving `PLAYER_MOVE_PRESENTATION_MS` owning only the compositor's glide. Because the question now opens ahead of the landing, the mid-commit subject is `promoting` rather than `arrived` and its eyebrow reads **Pawn promoting**: copy must not assert a landing the screen has not shown, which is the same standard ADR-0541 applied to the premove's will-become wording. The destination highlight and the terrain footstep do not move — the footstep still seats with the piece, not with the question. Everything else in ADR-0503 stands: the arrival remains presentation state and not a partial chess state, canonical `GameState` sits at the pre-move position until the choice supplies the single atomic `applyMove`, a lobby seat still submits one complete `{ destination, promotion }` intent after choice and keeps the local projection until the ordered echo, and rejection still removes it. No new animation, sound, speculative commit, or netplay rollback — the change removes a timer rather than adding one. No document, database, or save-format change | accepted; partially supersedes [0503](0503-pawns-arrive-before-their-promotion-choice.md)'s glide-gated choice clause; refines ADR-0504/0541 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0560](0560-main-menu-marks-share-one-ink-box-and-one-centre.md) | Every main-menu mark is fitted to ONE ink HEIGHT and ONE centre line: its ink scaled to exactly **52px tall** on ADR-0026's 64x64 canvas, both ink dimensions pinned EVEN, and the ink box centred on both axes from its own pixels. Height and NOT the long axis, which is the second thing tried and the reason this ADR states a measurement: pinning the long axis equalizes SIZE but not vertical PADDING, because a mark wider than it is tall spends the box on its width and comes back short — measured on the live rail that was 14.6px of padding above and below the wide three-in-a-row Lobbies mark against 5.6px on its neighbours, and 6.3px on the slightly-wide Editor. The rail stacks five marks in a column against a shared button frame, so the gap above and below each mark is what the eye compares, and pinning the height makes it identical for every mark by construction: measured 5.625px above and below, every mark, every set. A subject too wide to fit at that height is REFUSED and named rather than shrunk, since shrinking it is exactly the unequal padding the rule removes — which is why Lobbies is three upright marks and not three in a row (108px wide at 52 tall). This retires ADR-0027 §C's per-shape optical KEYLINE table (blade 56, route-map 52, scroll 48, pawns 48, gear 52) for this set only; keylines equalize optical MASS and are right for a dense functional set read as scattered symbols, but the installed marks measured 33x56, 52x49, 48x40, 40x40 and 52x50, putting a 40px glyph directly above a 56px one. Even dimensions matter because an odd one against an even canvas resolves half a pixel off and leaves an extra row of margin on one side. Two SEPARATE causes of the mark sitting high are fixed beside the fit, both found by measuring the rendered rail rather than reading the file: `.main-menu-mode-tab .settings-tab-icon img` resolved its half-pixel centring UPWARD to keep the raster on whole logical pixels, which bought nothing (44/64 is already fractional, so the art resamples either way) and made the gap above 6px and below 7px on every button; and both rail heights were computed against a 2px border — "border(4) + 2*padding + icon slot(40)" — while the panel-line border-image resolves to **7px** a side, leaving a 30px content box around a 40px icon row that overflowed the whole 10px DOWNWARD, seating every mark and label 5px below its button centre line on every rail in the family. `.settings-tab` now centres its track with `align-content: center` rather than a re-derived padding number, because the border-image width is the thing that already moved once; Settings, Editor, Play, Enchiridion and the Strategikon rails all correct together. `pack-menu-icons.mjs` verifies the bytes it writes with the same `inkBounds` primitive `trim-icon-margin.mjs` and the title-bar seat gate use, failing when the ink is not exactly 52px tall or when top/bottom or left/right margins differ AT ALL. The set also leaves dark blue stone, which is ADR-0433's STRUCTURAL FIELD material and the opposite of what a menu mode button is — a leaf control wearing the material of the surface under it — and ADR-0035 already forbade a forced UI-blue for semantic icons; each mark is then drawn in the materials ITS OWN object is made of — steel and brass on the sword, parchment and wood on the map, bone and ebony on the pawns, leather and brass on the handbook, iron on the cog — which is ADR-0035's standing per-image rule and the precedent the kit's wooden `lyre` beside its bronze `bell` already set. Recorded because the first pass got it backwards and regenerated the marks as ONE material across all five (a limestone set, a bronze set, a pewter set), which is the same constraint the retired blue stone imposed in a different hue: what holds this set together is the canvas, the ink height, the centre line and the low-fi treatment, so a uniform material across the set is a defect and not a style. Enchiridion stops borrowing the shared kit blueprint glyph other surfaces also draw and gets `ui/main-menu/icons-carved/enchiridion.png`. The SET is the unit of review and installation, since a mark can only be judged against the four it stands beside: candidates mount as whole rails through the real `ApparatusRailColumn`/`ApparatusRailTab` at `/studio?menuIconReview=1`, and installing accepts every slot plus the one drawable re-point in one act — review one call PER VERSION, acceptance one batch, because a review batch is only for versions sharing a declared acceptance group and five independent slots are refused with `media_review_batch_requires_one_acceptance_group`. A mark changes EVERYWHERE it is drawn or it has not changed: a destination's mark resolves through `menuModeIcon` and nowhere else, so the menu rail, the Strategikon section rail and the compact Strategikon index beside the Controls title all followed without an edit, which is the whole reason that resolver exists; the two consumers bypassing it are now routed through it (`PlayMenu` carried a byte-identical PRIVATE COPY of the lookup, and the Strategikon seat's compensation numbers in `style.css` named the old kit glyph by hand). The gear is the case the resolver does not cover, since most gears are not the Settings DESTINATION — the Battle HUD's Controls tab, the Settings General section and `.icon-gear` all draw `ui/kit/icons/gear.png` — so the chosen gear is installed into BOTH its menu slot and that kit slot, one set of bytes in two slots, while the title-bar cluster gear and the shell's cold-load ladder read the menu-mode drawable and follow on their own. What deliberately did NOT move is `ui/kit/icons/design-index.png`: it looks like the Enchiridion's old mark but is also the Level Editor tab's mark in `PlayMenu` and the *open ground* terrain bullet in the Enchiridion's own rules copy, so overwriting it would have changed two unrelated things. Installing also required lifting these slots out of `ui-kit`'s bridge-only default with a `mainMenuMarkMediaIssue` typed completeness validator (registered slot, ui-kit domain, image/png, 64x64 canvas, declared `inkHeight: 52` and even ink dimensions — what the rail depends on is geometry, so geometry is what the backend checks), and recording the fit honestly: the packer RESAMPLES, so the versions declare `native1x: false, spatialResampling: true` with the exact transform under a declared exception schema rather than claiming a native 1x the bytes do not have (ADR-0076), pinning the SHAPE and not the output hashes as ADR-0332 did, because this family is expected to be regenerated and a hash-pinned gate becomes a gate nobody runs. Accepted cost: one height does not equalize optical MASS, and the rule constrains what a mark's arrangement may be — the lever is the subject, never a per-icon target number | accepted; supersedes ADR-0027 §C's keyline table for the main-menu set; refines ADR-0026/0035/0433 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0556](0556-run-preparation-chooses-in-a-tab-column-of-one-line-rows.md) | Run preparation's choice column is a **tab column** at `--col-tab-w`, and its rows are one line in the main-menu button's 61px seat. The shell has two column types and the action width is arithmetic, not a design value — *"the MAX that fits beside two tab columns at the 1240 body cap: 1240 − 2×322 − 2×12 = 572px"* — which was right while Run sat THIRD in its row. ADR-0514 deleted the Play mode rail and moved the choices into the first destination seat, deciding only that they must not inherit the narrow-viewport compression; the leftover width was never re-decided, so Current Run and Start New Run kept 546px rows beside 346px of empty body at a width nobody chose. Sized as what they are — a column that SELECTS, with the detail column beside it doing the acting — the rows land byte-identical to the main-menu buttons from the same variable. `--col-action-w` is untouched and still correct for the columns that genuinely act (Levels, Campaign Levels, Continue); this is a Run-scoped class on the shared column, not a third width (ADR-0059). Both second lines went with it: Current Run's `Battle 3 of 10 · Ataraxia 0` is the first two facts of the detail column the row opens, restated one click early, and Start New Run's `Choose Ataraxia` names the control immediately to its right — together buying a 96px slab for a row whose content is a name. ADR-0334's **No active Run** survives as the row's optional END VALUE rather than a second line, because it is the only thing that says why the row cannot be taken; both states are then one line at a constant height, which is load-bearing rather than cosmetic, since the two rows are cut from ONE plank of installed oak by a fixed `--play-choice-row-surface-pitch` (ADR-0034/ADR-0063) and a row that grew a line when a Run ended would shift the plank beneath it. The two stacks are byte-identical rather than merely similar: the seat is a FIXED `block-size: 61px` like the tab's, not a `min-height` the clamp()ed copy grows past (55.83px at 1280vw, 61.86px at 1920vw), and the list reads the rail's own `--main-menu-tab-column-gap` rather than the shared 10px row gap, which disagreed with the rail's clamp from the first gap onward — measured equal top, bottom, height, gap and pitch at 1280, 1600 and 1920, so both terms of the plank pitch are exact and the sheet no longer drifts at the extremes. The **RUN** eyebrow goes with them: a section title distinguishes one row group from the others beside it, and this column holds exactly one group, so it labelled the whole column with the name of the only thing in it — the section keeps its accessible name, only the drawn label goes, and the first row then starts at the column's own top inset, measured level with the first main-menu button at every width. Presentation only — no document, database, or save-format change | accepted; refines ADR-0290/0334/0514 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0557](0557-the-two-active-runs-question-is-answered-behind-current-run.md) | When a browser and its account each hold a different active Run, the question of which one the account keeps is answered BEHIND Current Run, in the detail column that row opens, instead of by a card standing in the row's seat. ADR-0334 had already decided the principle — a choice row is an availability surface, not an existence surface, and stays put disabled rather than disappearing — but carved out this one exception, and the exception cost exactly what the rule prevents: Run preparation offers two things and in this state one of them was simply gone, handing the player a question in place of the control they came for. It is also a question most players in that moment do not have, since the code already records that an adoption conflict does not gate a new Run — starting one discards both candidates and is a THIRD answer rather than something blocked by the question — so anyone heading for Start New Run was made to read a disambiguation their next click resolves anyway. The row is now present in every state; the conflict replaces the Current Run FACTS rather than the row, so taking Current Run asks which Run before it offers to play one, with **Keep account Run** / **Adopt browser Run** in the ordinary two-column detail action row where Play and Start Run sit. It is also stated ONCE: the store stops writing a companion string into the shared `persistenceError` channel (`This browser and account each have an active Run` on hydrate, `Choose which active Run this account should keep` on a save conflict), because `adoptionConflict` is the state and that channel renders under the choice list — beside a Start New Run the conflict has never gated. It is no longer announced as an `alert` either, since a question you navigate to is not an interruption; a player who never opens Current Run is simply never told, which is the point. The channel keeps its remaining messages — waiting cloud sync, a failed abandon, an unsupported save version — which no other surface reports. ADR-0334's exception is withdrawn, which is what makes its visible-but-disabled language a rule rather than a default. Presentation only — the store's conflict detection, `keepAccountRun`, `adoptBrowserRun` and the save format are untouched | accepted; refines ADR-0290/0334/0556 | 2026-08-09 |
| [0558](0558-a-menu-language-rail-tab-is-the-primitive-or-it-fails-the-build.md) | Every menu-language rail button in the app is `ApparatusRailTab` and every rail column is `ApparatusRailColumn`, enforced by `check-rail-tab-primitive.mjs` in both `npm run check` and `npm run build`, with **no exemptions**. The primitive already existed and already worked — mount a tab and the column owns width, gap, placement and material while the tab owns seat, mark canvas, label fitting, active state and its slice of the continuous stone — and FOUR surfaces hand-assembled their own anyway: Settings' section tabs, the Campaign Editor's campaign tabs, its editor collection tabs, and Run preparation's Current Run / Start New Run, each from `chromeUnitClassNames('inner-box', 'settings-tab main-menu-mode-tab', …)` plus a `--tab-index` prop plus a hand-built icon span. Each looked right in isolation, which is the failure mode; a lookalike is only visibly wrong beside the thing it copies. Run is the worked example — same width, same left inset, same first-row top, then a `10px` gap against the rail's `clamp(9px, 0.8vw, 13px)` and a `min-height` seat the clamp()ed copy grew past (55.83px at 1280vw, 61.86px at 1920vw) against the tab's flat `block-size: 61px`, so 2.76px out by the second row at 1600×900 and 5.4px at 1280×800, equal near 1250vw only by coincidence. ADR-0059 already said reuse the canonical primitive; availability is not enforcement. What a surface needs, the primitive GROWS: `disabled`, `locked`, `trailing`, `onSelect`, `ariaLabel` and a non-navigating `role="button"` host all arrived by converting a lookalike back in and are now available to every rail rather than to the screen that built them. That host is a host choice and not a second tab — identical classes, seat, mark, copy and states — for the two tabs that cannot use the nav control, since the Campaign Editor seats an interactive favourite at its trailing edge which cannot nest inside a button, and the collection tabs select without an address. The dressing rooms stay owners because they generate CSS TARGETING these classes for retuning rather than building a tab. Run's destinations therefore carry marks like every other destination (the Battle mark, the Ataraxia emblem the title bar and Enchiridion already use), and measure identical to the rail beside them at 1280/1600/1920 — not by two stacks agreeing but because there is one stack. `.play-run-choice-col`, its gap override, `.play-choice-row` and `--play-choice-row-surface-pitch` all delete; the plank is the rail's single `--settings-tab-surface-pitch`, and `settingsRailContinuity.test.ts` now expects zero direct renderers outside the primitive. Presentation only — no document, database, or save-format change | accepted; refines ADR-0059/0231/0556 | 2026-08-10 |
| [0559](0559-run-rail-tabs-wear-their-own-marks.md) | Run's Current Run and Start New Run tabs wear their OWN installed marks — a chess king and a sealed parchment, subjects chosen by the owner — on new roles `ui-kit-icons-run-current-png` / `ui-kit-icons-run-new-png`, replacing the borrowed title-bar Battle and Ataraxia marks that read as arbitrary because they are minted for the bar's tight measure chip, authored edge-to-edge so they need `markCanvas="bleed"`, and warm where the kit is not. No palette constraint: the colour-devoid style is retired (a first spread was generated against that dead rule and was wrong wholesale), leaving only the seat's constraints — the ADR-0026 64×64 kit canvas and legibility at the 44px the rail draws. Acceptance refused them outright, which is the system working: `ui-kit` candidates stay bridge-only until their slot family has a typed completeness validator, so `runRailMarkMediaIssue` states one — and it is the OPPOSITE of a title-bar mark's, since a title-bar mark must be TRIMMED to its ink (its square seat scales the canvas) while a rail mark must RESERVE the kit canvas margin, that margin being its optical centring, so trimming would draw it larger than the kit icons beside it. Review happens in the seat that ships: `RunRailMarkCatalog` is a Studio category mounting every candidate on a real `ApparatusRailTab` at native size, because reviewing in the measure chip reviews a size they are never drawn at and a contact sheet is not a review. A candidate that cannot be accepted is not offered — the seat filters to versions carrying provenance, native-1x evidence, a measured ink box and the typed runtime projection, so no card looks installable and then fails with a server code. The ink band is 62–95% rather than the kit's authored 62–84%: generated art lands nearer the edge and the seat compensates with a stated ink fraction; the band rejects only what the seat cannot compensate. Production content change — two new slots, additive and retirable | accepted; refines ADR-0026/0558 | 2026-08-10 |
| [0561](0561-a-rail-tab-marks-what-it-opened-on-the-press.md) | A rail tab that EXPANDS a panel wears a `›` open mark at its trailing edge, bound to the intended address rather than the committed one, so it appears in the same animation frame as the press instead of 383–1441ms later when the destination's crossfade finishes. The cyan `is-active` ring keeps the committed clock ([ADR-0369](0369-one-cold-load-ladder-builds-background-then-chrome-then-scene.md): chrome making a claim about what is ON SCREEN must not run ahead of it) — a rail button is answering a different question, "which one did I just press", and for the length of a fade it had been answering with the tab the player pressed AWAY from, or still insisting an already-collapsed panel was open. `active` and `expanded` are therefore separate props on `ApparatusRailTab` ([ADR-0558](0558-a-menu-language-rail-tab-is-the-primitive-or-it-fails-the-build.md)), and only a tab that opens something takes the second.
