The required opening frame shares the playable grid's centre, so a box centred here survives `normalizeBoardCameraBounds` still centred — an undersized box grows symmetrically instead of being shoved off the centre it was just given. **Snap to resolution** resizes to exactly that many world pixels about the centre already in hand: world pixels are screen pixels at zoom 1, so a box the size of a display is what that display sees fully zoomed out with the art drawn one-to-one, and stating that previously meant dragging a handle until Size happened to read 1920 × 1080. Presets are real display sizes grouped by aspect family, authored in `boardCameraBounds.ts`; a box the opening frame forced larger reports what it actually got rather than the resolution asked for. The two compose in either order and neither does the other's job — centring never resizes, snapping never recentres — and both take the same normalization, undo, autosave and persistence path as every other camera write. | accepted; refines [0301](0301-levels-own-an-authored-camera-coverage-boundary.md), [0491](0491-camera-boundary-can-adopt-the-current-editor-view.md) | 2026-08-12 |
| [0584](0584-a-capture-that-gives-check-is-paid-for-the-tempo.md) | **A capture that gives check is paid for the tempo, and only when it hangs nothing — one Manubium, `capture-with-check`, at ten gold.** The owner asked for it in a sentence and amended it before it shipped: taking a piece and putting the King in check in the same move should get a bonus, *while not attacked*. Every entry before it paid for something standing on the board — material won, the shape of a check, the reach of a line, how little is giving mate — and none paid for the one thing this move buys, which is a MOVE: the enemy must answer the check rather than whatever they meant to do about the capture. The amendment is what makes it that advice rather than its opposite, because a capture with check that leaves the capturing unit hanging is a donation dressed up as a tempo move — they answer the check by taking it. Three clauses, all required: the move took an enemy unit, the enemy King is in check on the committed board, and the unit that TOOK is not attacked where it now stands. The first two are properties of the MOVE and not of a unit, so a capture that opens a line behind it earns it exactly as one that checks from where it lands, and no before-and-after comparison is needed because the enemy King could not already have been in check on the player's turn. The third asks only about the capturer, the one unit standing somewhere new; a checker that was already where it was is not asked about, since the check must be answered first. `unitIsAttacked` — a new mirror of the existing `unitIsDefended` in `core/rules`, reading the same `attacksSquare` geometry — is the bar, so it is ATTACK GEOMETRY and not legality: a pinned attacker counts, and so does an enemy King eyeing a square it may not enter because the unit is defended. That is deliberately stricter than the fork's `forkHolds`, which asks whether taking the forker would COST the enemy more than it is worth: a fork that can be taken never collects its second prong, while here the material is already in hand, so the question is not whether the exchange is good but whether anything was left in the open. Ten gold at ADR-0540's noticing pole beside the long capture and the royal fork — the capture is still one the player wanted anyway and the safety clause keeps the entry from teaching a donation rather than making it dear. It stacks with everything including the mate, and is seated on the square the capture landed on. Board law, RunSaveVersion, save shape and the database are untouched. | accepted | 2026-08-11 |
| [0586](0586-an-observing-capture-declares-itself-on-the-request-that-creates-a-document.md) | **An observing capture declares itself on the request that CREATES a document, not only on the one that joins it.** `npm run shot` called itself an observer and CLAUDE.md promised screenshots "never write" to a document — but every capture of an editor URL carrying no `levelId` and no `document` minted a durable working copy (`user/campaign`, `l70`-style id, **Untitled level**, revision 1) and then observed the thing it had just made; fifteen were sitting on the owner's account, surfacing in the **Continue editing** list ADR-0090 scopes. Observation had been fitted to the join and never to the birth: `installObservationSessionPatch` rewrites the edit-session open to `intent: 'observe'` and the server honours it, but a session-open needs a document id, so the write happens one request earlier in `POST /api/editor-documents/resolve` — one address doing two jobs, attaching to a working copy and creating one for a URL that has none. Measured live, not argued: `?levelId=off-l-hold-bridge` resolves **200** onto the existing `legacy-j5kip7ztaipw` and `dbReconcileEditorDocument` writes nothing, while a bare `/editor/level` resolves **201** and mints. Resolve now parses the same `intent` field the session-open has always parsed (body or `x-level-editor-session-intent`, `write` default); under `observe` a create-shaped body is **409 `observation_cannot_create_editor_document`**, a `level_id` with no working copy is **404 `editor_document_not_found_for_level`** thrown before the INSERT, and an existing copy resolves unchanged. `editorDocumentObservationKind` is the one classifier the page rewrite and its node-side tally share, and `shot.mjs` reports a refusal BEFORE writing pixels — the editor falls back to a local-only copy when its document never arrives, so the selector would have been found and the PNG would have looked like a successful capture of a level that did not exist. `--anonymous` was fixed in the same pass because the error message now points at it: it skipped only the sign-in navigation while the dev backend grants a loopback request an owner session anyway, and its signed-out mock sat inside CDP interception that runs only behind `--abort-request*`. No migration; `intent` defaults to `write` | accepted; refines [0090](0090-private-draft-cards-preview-and-manage-working-copies.md), [0304](0304-level-editor-documents-are-live-shared-working-copies.md) | 2026-08-12 |
| [0585](0585-abandoning-a-run-is-confirmed-in-the-seat-that-raised-it.md) | **Abandoning a Run is confirmed in the seat that raised it, by ONE control mounted in three places.** `useRunAbandon` opened a modal through `useConfirm` — an `alertdialog` portalled to `<body>` over a dimmed screen — while the SAME question is answered inline one screen away: Start New Run states the stakes in a cell of its own box and ARMS its verb into Keep Run / Abandon and Start ([ADR-0571](0571-start-new-run-is-one-box-of-cells.md)). Two ways to ask "are you sure you want to lose this Run?" one screen apart is the parallel [ADR-0059](0059-reuse-the-canonical-primitive-not-a-bespoke-parallel.md) forbids, and the popup was the worse of them: it asked the question somewhere other than where it was raised, over the Run under discussion; it drew its answers in `le-seg-btn`, a chrome nothing else on the screen wears; and it spent a heading ("Abandon this Run?") restating the button just pressed. The stakes are one sentence and never needed a layer. `RunAbandonControl` now owns both states — at rest the danger verb wearing the sign-out mark and keeping `data-testid="abandon-run"` exactly where it stood; armed, a `run-abandon-keep` / `run-abandon-confirm` pair sharing ONE row, because they are two answers to one question and stacking reads as two things to do. Neither answer wears a mark: the mark belongs to the control that ASKED. **The armed question is ONE BOX holding those two answers** — a `DividedInnerChromeBox` on the structural marble, `verbColumns(answers)` declaring its two tracks, `ChromeVerbRow` seating them — so the line between them is a rail the box lays and caps from its own grid lines ([ADR-0242](0242-divided-inner-grids-own-one-rail-topology.md)), capped where it meets the frame at either end. Shipped first as two framed buttons in a flex row with a gap, which is wrong twice over — a gap is what this kit puts between things that are NOT related, and each button drew a second frame just inside the one the question needed. The box takes no padding or its rail stops short of the frame it has to reach, and the cells that ARE the controls take none either. **ONE seat height through both states**, because pressing a control must not resize the thing under the cursor: `--run-abandon-seat-h` is stated once and derived twice — the resting button takes it directly, the box's frameless plate is it less a rail a side — so the box lands at the same height AND its wood on exactly the wood the button painted. 46px is read off the live DOM rather than chosen (every button in all three rails measures it, 7px frame, 32px oak), and BOTH states are pinned to it because the resting height was emergent — a 38px floor plus the mark's line box — so a font or mark change would have moved one and not the other, silently; the rail comes from `--le-chrome-inner-rail-w`, which chromeFamilyRuntime stamps from the installed catalog, so a retuned frame moves the plate with it. Shipped wrong twice on the way here: a 30px plate (44px box against a 46px button) and then a 26px one derived from a 40px `min-block-size` that was only a FLOOR the mark already exceeded. Verified live in all three rails: resting and armed both 46.00px to the hundredth. **It states NO stakes.** The sentence naming what abandoning costs went with the dialog: a modal that has darkened the screen owes the reader a reason for doing so and its body text IS that reason, while a control answering in its own seat opens nothing and owes nothing — the warning was the dialog's body outliving its dialog, telling a player who just pressed Abandon Run what they had already decided. It cost a full turn of alignment argument first (left vs centre vs justify, a bare text node taking one of the box's two tracks, a rag orphaning "This") before the answer turned out to be that the sentence should not be there — the House rule that fell out of the survey stands and is worth keeping: centre a control's LABEL, a readout in its own seat, or a message owning its whole surface; left-align prose sharing a box with anything else, and never justify (0 uses in-tree, and none wanted at a ~290px measure in a pixel face). Without the sentence the seat does not move on arming at all. Armed, the SEAT has no say in material: a box establishes its own containment level, marble field and oak on every cell that is a control, in all three seats. The modal's two real safeties are kept rather than dropped — focus lands on Keep Run, Escape keeps the Run. The control existed THREE times before (the Sectio rail, the Deployment panel, and a third built inside the HUD from an `onAbandonRun` callback, markless and at a different measure), so `SkirmishHudProps` takes `abandonRun?: ReactNode` and is handed the CONTROL: the confirmation happens in the seat, so the seat is what the Run contributes, passed as a module-constant element because the Battle presentation is memoized against unrelated Run writes. Material stays the SEAT's, not the control's ([ADR-0433](0433-leaf-chrome-uses-oak-over-structural-teal-fields.md)) — `fillSurface` defaults to the leaf oak and the HUD's copy passes `null`, since its Controls panel never adopted the hierarchy and every button beside it wears the field. `useConfirm` keeps its other consumers (Resign, Level Editor, Campaign editor); this retires it for the Run's own destructive verb, not as a primitive. Verified on the live route with REAL mouse clicks in all three seats: the arming press has to wait for `data-scene-phase="current"` or it lands on the transition layer and nothing arms (the same trap ADR-0571's check hit), a real click on the armed Abandon Run lands on `/play/select/run` reporting **No active Run**, and a real Keep Run and a real Escape each return the resting control with the Run intact. The armed seat is taller than the resting one and grows on the press — deliberately NOT ADR-0571's reserved-space case, which reserves room for a disclosure nobody opens so the column cannot shift under a player who never touches it. No gameplay, RunSaveVersion, save-shape or database change; `abandon()` is untouched, [ADR-0544](0544-leaving-a-run-does-not-wait-for-its-account-row.md) included | accepted; refines [0571](0571-start-new-run-is-one-box-of-cells.md)/[0242](0242-divided-inner-grids-own-one-rail-topology.md)/[0433](0433-leaf-chrome-uses-oak-over-structural-teal-fields.md)/[0059](0059-reuse-the-canonical-primitive-not-a-bespoke-parallel.md) | 2026-08-12 |
| [0587](0587-the-controls-head-is-one-divided-block.md) | **The Controls head is one divided block, standing on the panel's own marble and drawn in inner-box chrome.** The name band was the last unpressable WOODEN surface in the app, which under ADR-0433 promises a control the plate is not, and the line under it was a bolted forged strip baked into the title element's own `background` — an earlier iteration's private section rule, from before this panel had section breaks, and by then the only line in the app not drawn by a `ChromeDivider`. The band now declares no surface at all, so the panel's installed marble runs through it unbroken; painting marble there instead would restart the stone at the band's origin and seam against the body below. The name and the five HUD tabs are ONE divided block laid by `ShellControlsPanel`, built exactly like the title bar's invariant cluster (ADR-0569): a spanning name row, a strip of compartments, and a foot row that closes it against the body — so the rule under the name IS that block's row boundary and the caps where the strip's four verticals cross it are the block's own junctions. It shipped FIRST at the OUTER role — the panel's 24px section rail and its 32px studs — which was the right structure at the wrong weight; the owner rejected it on sight (*"im not picturing those huge frames being the ones we use"*) and named the settings/music/avatar cluster as the reference, which is the inner-box class. The block is unframed on its INLINE axis because it runs the full width of the panel: its left and right edges are the panel's own side rails, a second pair a rail-width inside them would trap a strip of marble between two frames, and the rails that reach them simply run under them. It also does not CLIP, which an unframed grid otherwise does so a shell workspace cannot drop its rails onto the chrome around it — wrong for a block whose boundary is its host's frame, since a rail on a boundary straddles it and the closing rail came out half-height with its four caps cut through the middle; it pays for that by not bleeding at all (`--chrome-divided-grid-reach: 0`), so the only thing outside its box is that rail, a few pixels into the gutter above the body, where the default reach would have painted a rail-width past the panel onto the board. A compartment is NOT a registered chrome unit — the unit is what brings the frame and the block already drew every edge this thing has: a pressable one wears the leaf oak, one that merely holds other people's controls stays the block's field, and which is CURRENT is carried by the mark inside it because a frameless compartment has no frame to light. Equal tracks do not give equal compartments, and TWO things are taken off a cell here: the half-rail on each internal side, and the panel's own FRAME sitting on top of the block's outer edges, which is by far the bigger bite and the one that showed — the first compartment painted **42px** of visible oak beside 64px ones, measured off a real capture while every box rect read a tidy 63.52. Each track adds back exactly what is taken from it, with the cover measured from the rail the panel actually draws so the viewport edge (where `chrome-rails-offscreen` draws none) states zero rather than assuming symmetry, and the matching insets come from the same `chromeDividedSeatAxis` call because the row's last child is the grid's own rail layer and `:last-child` is not the last seat. All five then paint 60/60/60/60/60 device pixels at 1440 with each mark centred on its own opening; the ±1px left at fractional column widths is the browser's own device-pixel rounding. Both head shapes are the same block: the Level Editor's layer picker is that block with one column and no foot, since a foot rail terminates the verticals BETWEEN compartments and one column has none. The name re-centres in a field 10px taller than the bolted strip left it and the Strategikon marks ride that same seat instead of being pinned to the top of it; measured against the app bar that IS the house seating — the brand lockup sits low because 54px of lockup in a 60px field has no slack to spend, not because a header is bottom-set, and the bottom-set variant was built and crowds name and marks into the rail. The head costs 104.7px against the framed row's 105.7px, `.skirmish-hud-tabs` and every declaration about tab width, gap and frame are gone, the retired oak leaves the surface-debt baseline rather than being re-registered, and `DividedInnerChromeBox` is untouched — the shape this needed was already in it. | accepted; refines [0433](0433-leaf-chrome-uses-oak-over-structural-teal-fields.md), [0569](0569-the-invariant-title-bar-cluster-is-one-divided-box.md) | 2026-08-12 |
